# Anatomy working notes

The [study manuscript](anatomy.md) is for grades **9–10, non-AP/non-Honors**. The [design brief](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md) owns lasting intent and constraints. The [source library](sources/README.md) records research provenance. This file records story ideas, arcs, hypotheses, reviews, measured results and decisions.

The textbook-spread audit comes first, followed by the embryonic-signal revision and the reordered-subject/cell revision, its preceding human item audit and the local-anatomy/final-assembly revision, followed by the physical-item revision, the enclosed-cell correction, the ordinary-biology revision, the preceding Fun revision, the opening-linearity review, naming record, cell-prelude development, textbook revision and preceding curriculum study. Earlier sections preserve the proposals, scores and judgments of their time, including superseded audiences, manuscripts and provisional conclusions. Their filenames and hashes identify historical conditions; the older playable manuscripts and separate note files have been removed. Requested player records and reports remain in `playtests/`.

<a id="textbook-spread-audit"></a>
## Anatomy textbook spreads — audit and revision, 14 November 2026

The user requested two changes—two arm receiving sites accepting mineral or muscle in either order, and actual embryonic sources/signals for the nerve-cell chapter—then requested a thorough audit of the conceit: **does every chapter teach a coherent chunk of anatomy/physiology that could sustain a textbook illustration?** This audit evaluates the actual items and recipes, not titles alone. Its illustration judgments concern proposed medical-textbook drawings; no graphics or screenshots were produced.

### Scope and independent review

The baseline was `35d00343db5a90e34b7730dfd2f535e1dee4f0898d3b1c98caf560300a34cb7e`. Two fresh reviewers independently audited chapters 6–16 and 17–29 from the manuscript, without prior notes or scores. A separate researcher checked concrete neural-development sources. A fresh reader reviewed revised chapters 1–5, and another independently reviewed the revised whole arc with particular attention to chapters 14, 16, 21–23 and 28–29. They received the textbook conceit, physical-item/scale requirements and ordinary-school audience; informed readers could consult the manuscript's sources. None was asked to invent Fun scores. Detailed instructions and raw replies were temporary; the adopted findings and limits are below.

The main finding is **representation needs more repair than chapter order**. Most subjects have distinct textbook questions. Ventilation, gas exchange and airway defense belong together but are not the same lesson. Timed pancreatic comparisons and tissue-to-organ locators are valid illustration devices. The failures were chiefly underdefined object boundaries, fluids renamed for travelling, vague signal sources and incomplete local systems left for the finale.

### Chapter-by-chapter audit

“Keep” means a defensible spread, not proof of student comprehension or rendered-art legibility. “Tune” includes identified illustration requirements; “rebuild” changes the actual interaction or object model.

| Chapter | Judgment and sensible chunk | Textbook illustration; adopted action or remaining requirement |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — An inside of its own** | **Keep.** What does a cellular boundary enclose, and where is DNA? | One cell with a nuclear enlargement. Independent outer/nuclear enclosures converge. The bag is explicitly not a complete living cell; no change. |
| **2 — The powerhouse of the cell** | **Keep.** How do cell structures support energy transfer and protein production? | One intact cell with connected respiration and ribosome enlargements. Glucose/ATP are supplies, ribosomes remain installed, RNA stays inside. No change. |
| **3 — Same DNA, different jobs** | **Tune context.** How do prepared embryonic cells make different tissue proteins? | Clearly separated early limb and later tendon scenes, with sources and recipient groups. Shh now has one reusable molecular representation; the scene supplies its source. Muscle proteins stay inside cells, tendon collagen goes outside. Vocabulary remains demanding and locally taught. |
| **4 — Hard bones and pulling muscles** | **Rebuild.** How do hard bone and differently attached muscles make opposite elbow pulls? | Two fixed regions of one arm. Each takes the same mineral and muscle-cell supplies in either order. Radius and ulna windows prevent mineralizing the same shared bone twice. Biceps/triceps remain distinct named muscles; no third bone hotspot or replacement-state chain. |
| **5 — Cells that send messages** | **Rebuild sources/recipients.** How do whole neurons and their separate wrapping cells develop and relate anatomically? | Early spinal-cord and later peripheral-nerve views lead to a complete motor-neuron model. Shh acts on spinal precursors; membrane-bound neuregulin-1 contacts Schwann precursors. Existing source axons avoid circular dependence. Continuous axon, separate wraps/nuclei, local channel enlargement. This is the densest early spread; artwork and actual student comprehension remain open. |
| **6 — Brain, spinal cord and nerves** | **Keep/tune.** How do central and peripheral structures connect and receive protection? | Head/back section plus a located root-junction enlargement. Clarified that the spinal cord ends above the bottom of the vertebral canal while lower roots continue. No graph change. |
| **7 — From light to sight** | **Tune.** How do optical and sensory structures cooperate? | Eye section and magnified retinal lining with optic-nerve continuation. Name the supplied nerve population as retinal cells; locate the retina at the back, not at lens scale. Preserve the two useful constructions. |
| **8 — Vibration into signals** | **Tune.** How does vibration reach sensory cells through different materials? | Ear section with a located cochlear tissue enlargement. Cells are attached to the moving partition; relative movement bends hairs. They must not float freely in a generic liquid chamber. |
| **9 — Head turns, eyes stay** | **Tune boundaries.** How do turning and weighted sensors support stable eyes/posture? | Two inner-ear sensor enlargements plus a separate brain/spinal response circuit. Central connections stay within those outlined organs, not inside the ear. Retain the sensor contrast. |
| **10 — Blood carries more than oxygen** | **Tune materials.** What makes blood a useful transport and repair material? | Blood sample with marrow maturation and haemoglobin enlargements. Show individual amino acids, and name the supplied plasma proteins rather than hiding them under nutrients/salts. No new production pathway. |
| **11 — Outward, across and back** | **Tune factual wording.** How do vessel walls support delivery, exchange and return? | Matched artery/vein sections, local capillary bed and limb muscle-pump inset. Red cells normally stay within vessels; the old “all blood cells” wording was false. Qualify the valve picture as a limb-vein example. Locators must distinguish scales. |
| **12 — A pump with two routes** | **Keep, with scale discipline.** Why are pulmonary/systemic circuits and coronary supply different routes? | Heart section, reduced double-circuit diagram and coronary enlargement. A whole circulation cannot be drawn at heart size. Keep the separate contributions; explicit scale labels remain an art requirement. |
| **13 — Air beside blood** | **Keep.** How do gases cross while air and blood remain separate? | Airway/lung locator and alveolar-capillary enlargement with distinct air, epithelial, vessel-wall and blood spaces. Independent airway/exchange constructions are coherent. |
| **14 — Making room for a breath** | **Tune object boundary.** How does chest movement ventilate perfused lungs? | Paired thoracic sections and a pleural-coupling inset. Rename the status-like result to **Heart and lungs**; systemic vessels continue beyond the cropped chest. Preserve independent chest and heart/lung projects. |
| **15 — The air we breathe** | **Keep/tune identity.** How do airway and alveolar defenses differ? | Located ciliated-airway and engulfing-cell enlargements. The bacterial-remains result explicitly names lung **white** cells. The short chapter has two real processes and need not merge with ventilation. |
| **16 — A return route for tissue fluid** | **Tune scale and ingredient.** How does tissue fluid reach venous blood? | Microscopic entry inset → body route locator → neck junction inset. The neck-vein input is an actual segment with an unmade lymph inlet, not a completed junction combined again. The route cannot be one microscopic local cutaway. |
| **17 — A response that remembers** | **Tune promised payoff.** How does a targeted response produce memory? | Lymph-node/cell enlargements with first/later encounters using the same antigen shape. Show surviving memory cells responding sooner. The existing chain remains causal rather than a set of independent projects; do not claim new Fun from a memory caption. |
| **18 — Breaking down a meal** | **Keep.** How do chemical digestion and fat dispersal differ? | Tract locator with separate mouth, stomach and intestinal magnifications. Preserve the praised gameplay and player text. Author instructions specify distinct catalysts at their appropriate sites, not one common enzyme soup. |
| **19 — Across the gut wall** | **Keep.** How does intestinal structure bring digested material into blood? | Tube → folds → villus → cellular boundary, with lumen, cells, tissue fluid and capillary lumen distinguished. Surface and blood-route constructions meet physically. No graph change. |
| **20 — Fuel between meals** | **Tune material accounting.** How do liver and muscle bridge intermittent meals? | Labelled fed/fasting pancreatic and tissue panels. Growing muscle glycogen must visibly draw down supplied free glucose; insulin contributes a message, not carbohydrate. Timed views are appropriate physiology, not an incoherent scene. |
| **21 — A body is a habitat** | **Keep and finish the local system.** How does colon structure coexist with microbial contents and recover material? | Colon-wall section and lumen enlargement. Keep wall construction and fermentation; move the upper-gut/colon join here through the supplied cecal junction. The finale receives a finished digestive tract. |
| **22 — Filter, then take back** | **Rebuild fluid identity.** Why recover useful material after filtration, and where does urine go? | Whole urinary-system view beside one located filter/tube enlargement. Preserve retained blood while producing filtrate, then compositionally different urine. Build ureters/bladder independently; trace the same urine through them without making a destination-labelled new substance. |
| **23 — Save water, replace water** | **Rebuild transport; retain as short payoff.** How do conservation and intake solve different water problems? | Two located kidney/gut panels. Concentrated urine is a real changed mixture; drinking water moves into an existing capillary and keeps its identity. This comparison is earned by chapters 19 and 22, not a new control-mechanism lesson. The independent water position must not be pre-completed in the kidney result picture. |
| **24 — A living surface** | **Keep.** How can skin resist water loss while remaining supplied, sensitive and able to sweat? | One skin block, surface-lipid enlargement and continuous gland duct. Existing text already distinguishes living lower cells from flattened outer cells; keep vessels below the epidermis. No new ingredient required. |
| **25 — A small breach** | **Keep.** How do platelets and protein threads stop bleeding before skin regrows? | Same skin block plus local vessel-break enlargement. Plug and fibrin are real independent constructions; the surface wound remains open. Threads must be shown forming at this injury, not as a manufactured graft. |
| **26 — Close and strengthen** | **Tune causal picture.** Why does repair need surface, support and blood supply? | Successive views of the same wound with independent growing tissues. Show the original damaged vessel wall repaired or its remnant sealed before the temporary clot disappears; a newly connected branch alone did not establish that. |
| **27 — Still becoming** | **Tune precision.** How can tissues persist while cells and material turn over? | Located spleen → blood-carried iron → marrow route beside skin renewal. A whole haemoglobin molecule has four iron-containing sites; one-site views must be labelled subunit details. Keep the two replacement chains. |
| **28 — All together** | **Tune sequence/boundaries.** How do completed anatomical systems occupy and connect within one body? | Exploded model with front/back or transparent views, continuous upper gut and posterior spine route. No new local gut construction here. Preserve regional/skin projects, existing organ handoffs and substantial final joins. This is a legitimate summary atlas spread. |
| **29 — Epilogue** | **Explicit coda, not another construction chapter.** What continues after the model is assembled? | Reuse the completed-body view. Label the actionless reflection as an epilogue; do not add a completion token or count it as another playable textbook spread. |

### Decisions and limits

The reviewers suggested merging the thin water comparison or retaining it as a short payoff. The selected revision keeps the comparison with two independent physical actions—renal production and movement of unchanged drinking water—so its contrast remains playable without an invented product. The epilogue is explicitly labelled rather than forced into another construction. Three-goal acknowledgments remain where required by story syntax; they are author metadata, not player prose, so removing them merely because a reviewer saw them in Markdown would be inappropriate.

The fresh revised-manuscript reviewer found no blocking biological/arc defect but caught a picture-state error: producing concentrated urine depicted the independent water crossing already happening. R2 corrects that picture, leaving water position governed only by its placement. Stale numerical cross-references in skin/lymph author notes are replaced with stable chapter IDs. No correction is treated as evidence that finished illustrations already work.

### Validation and score comparison

Selected source: **`d6920c636ce31af51a80b341237c1ba3375d29daf23b71834aeb2eae8ff63229` (R2)**, with **127 reference-route combinations plus one ordinary transport placement, 28 playable chapters and an epilogue, 156 recipes and 254 items**. R1 was `4b72d35cda79000ff86b13d84d5c622e59d046201d7bc1c0a6a85eef533df6bc`. R2 changes the water-result picture and author-only scope/cross-reference notes; its recipe graph and all goals are identical to R1. Other R1 chapter results remain the latest applicable scoped evidence, not retests of the complete R2 manuscript. Chapter 18's player-facing text and recipes remain byte-identical to baseline; only an author-only illustration instruction was appended.

Four independent paired campaigns used the unchanged ordinary-tenth-grade guidance and actual Terra Medium for players, individual graders and synthesis. Each tested chapter/mode has three fresh players. The baseline tested chapters 4, 5, 17, 21, 22, 23 and 28; R1 tested those plus 3, 14 and 16; R2 retested 23; a rejected R3 tested chapter 4. Additional R1 profiles without a fresh baseline in this pass:
- Chapter 3: detailed Fun 2.3/4, Worthwhile 4/4, hooks 3H; fast Worthwhile 4/4, hooks 1M, 2H.
- Chapter 14: detailed Fun 1/4, Worthwhile 3/4, hooks 1M, 2H; fast Worthwhile 3.3/4, hooks 3M.
- Chapter 16: detailed Fun 1/3, Worthwhile 3/3, hooks 3M; fast Worthwhile 3/3, hooks 3M.

**What the observations support.** Specific neural sources/recipients retained chapter 5's 2/4 Fun, 4/4 Worthwhile and high detailed hooks while correcting its representation. Finishing the digestive tract in chapter 21 improved both Fun and Worthwhile in this sample; removing that preparatory join from the finale also improved its detailed profile. The colon and finale remain separate anatomical questions rather than one forced long build.

**What did not improve.** The requested four-fitting arm now offers all orders, but the second mineral application repeats a lesson: Fun's numerator stayed 2 while the denominator rose from 3 to 4; Worthwhile stayed 3 with that extra action. This is a density regression, not a Fun improvement. The source retains the user's requested two-site structure and records the repeated-material pattern as unresolved. Immune Fun fell 1.3/5 → 1/5 even as Worthwhile rose. Kidney Fun's numerator fell 2 → 1.7 while one transport-token action was removed; its fraction improved slightly, which is not an increase in credited decisions. Final fast hook acquired one low judgment despite stronger detailed results. None is hidden by an aggregate success claim.

**R2 hypothesis and outcome.** Keeping the kidney result from pre-completing the independent water route should make placement less redundant. Fast Worthwhile recovered from R1's 1/2 to 1.7/2; detailed Fun/Worthwhile remained 1/2 and 2/2. This is still below baseline fast 2/2, and players still found the immediate placement confirmation thin. Keep the factual/state correction and unchanged-water identity; do not restore a fictitious transported-water product to buy a popup. Finished-art feedback remains untested.

**R3 hypothesis and rejection.** A fresh four-action arm version postponed its bending/straightening demonstrations until each site's mineral and muscle were both present. Source `34ea4a67778d637b92b07d98ec97169bd9380bdb12a0bae5c75f584b08acdcd6`. Fast Worthwhile rose 3/4 → 3.7/4, but detailed Fun fell 2/4 → 1.7/4 and detailed Worthwhile stayed 3/4. The second construction still felt repetitive. Reject R3 rather than selecting only its favorable metric. The general lesson is that moving feedback alone does not repair a repeated construction pattern.

**Payoff rhythm and remaining limits.** Chapter 14's ventilation comparison is earned by preceding heart/circuit and lung-surface work. Chapter 17's causal antibody/memory chain follows the airway-defense and lymph-route projects in 15–16; its detailed value improved, but its fast 2/5 Worthwhile remains weak. Chapter 23 is a short conservation/intake comparison earned by the gut-wall and kidney projects in 19 and 22. Chapter 28 brings independently understood systems together and may legitimately become more directed at its final joins. These are explicit payoff roles, not excuses for making the opening or routine preparation linear. Chapters 3 and 5 remain the largest early vocabulary risks; model role-play does not establish actual student comprehension.

All eight campaigns passed original-provider-response matching, frozen native replay, individual-grade arithmetic, model/fresh-thread provenance, audience-guidance and report checks: **114 completed players, 114 independent grades, 38 syntheses, 510 recorded actions and 776 accepted fresh tool-free model requests**; zero retries, protocol rejections or extra look refreshes. Runtime hash `85fa5b68e5cc263b54813f60738100f2d5a4fcc3d19489c4604bff8363b51375`; guidance hash `2f369fe0c06b828b5ff33cf67527e5ef7f570902727c01771b976ccea2400656`. Requested arrays and reports remain in the project. Temporary source/runtime/review artifacts are discarded after verification.

Native lint reports **zero errors and six reviewed mixed-fate warnings**; the former fixed-state analysis-limit warning is absent. Native reference completion succeeds. Every one of the 24 arm-fitting orders, five legal neural-development/fitting orders, twelve kidney-processing/drainage orders and two water-comparison orders was checked through full-story completion: **43 routes**. These checks use R1's graph, unchanged in selected R2. Water's ordinary placement is present in the native route and completed by the text players, not merely proposed. The rendered blood-side landing still awaits artwork.

Retests concentrate on changed choices, dependencies, useful information and payoffs. Narrow terminology/precision corrections in 6–11, 15, 20 and 26–27 keep the same operations, goals and already disclosed relationships; source/illustration review was proportionate, with no new Fun score claimed. Chapters 1–2, 13, 18–19 and 24–25 preserve player-facing gameplay. No screenshot, graphics, engine change or cumulative human playthrough was performed. The roughly 200-combination ambition remains an ongoing project rather than a claim for this 127-combination revision.


<a id="embryonic-signals"></a>
## Named signals in an embryonic setting — 14 November 2026

The user proposed grounding the messenger items in a pictured embryo, with real molecule names and visible sources. Selected manuscript: `35d00343db5a90e34b7730dfd2f535e1dee4f0898d3b1c98caf560300a34cb7e`; matched baseline: `c4da372476c51be7476ba165ee05f42622456d24351c11f23b8d51b9b04ed33c`. Chapter 3 retains its four operations and two independent projects. The chapter order, recipes, fates and completion requirements remain unchanged; the native reference route still has **128 combinations**, with 28 playable chapters and ending 29.

### Hypothesis and grounding

**Hypothesis:** an actual place, time and molecule can make the same differentiation pattern more worthwhile without making its independent projects harder to understand. A generic messenger named after its desired outcome did not explain who produced it or why it belonged in the scene.

The selected example has two textbook views: earlier, Sonic hedgehog from the posterior limb-bud ZPA acts on selected muscle precursors; later, TGF-beta2 associated with developing cartilage, muscle and tendon cells helps tendon precursors continue developing. The supporting-tissue result is now specifically **Tendon tissue**. Muscle retains pulling proteins inside cells; tendon cells organize collagen outside themselves. The shared **Embryonic cells** item depicts distinct prepared groups, not interchangeable mature cells. Chapter 4 explicitly returns to anatomical model assembly, and chapter 5 introduces other embryonic groups. All images remain written plans.

One fresh source-informed reviewer, with no inherited history or scores, checked the proposed scene and the two primary papers. Its scope was science and teaching demand, not enjoyment. It supported the setting and required three distinctions, adopted before playtesting: Shh's demonstrated early target is a developmental regulator, not a contractile protein; TGF-beta supports tendon-progenitor maintenance rather than initial induction; and these mouse experiments are not direct human embryonic timelines. Result pictures now show time passing before downstream RNA/protein states. The [reading guide](sources/reading-guide.md#embryonic-limb-signals) preserves sources and limits, including proposed recruitment and unresolved relative contributions of the TGF-beta sources.

The new protein/location names are outside the ordinary-school vocabulary audit. Although the reviewer judged that names alone do not constitute a molecular-pathway lesson, the curriculum conservatively records this specific source–protein–recipient comparison as an **additional user-requested bounded exception** alongside the existing four detours. No regulatory gene names or receptor cascade is introduced to players. The first two chapters are unchanged.

### Matched chapter-3 comparison

Both conditions used the unchanged ordinary-tenth-grade guidance, three fresh actual Terra Medium players per mode, one fresh Terra Medium grader per record and a fresh synthesis per condition. Fast and detailed cohorts are separate. Baseline and candidate were frozen before testing; no outcome was supplied to a participant.

| Condition and report | Fun | Worthwhile | Independent hooks |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Baseline fast](playtests/2026.11.14_11.47.53/chapter3_report.md) | Not measured | 3.3/4 | 3 high |
| [Selected fast](playtests/2026.11.14_11.48.23/chapter3_report.md) | Not measured | 4/4 | 3 high |
| [Baseline detailed](playtests/2026.11.14_11.47.54/chapter3_report.md) | 2/4 | 3/4 | 3 high |
| [Selected detailed](playtests/2026.11.14_11.48.24/chapter3_report.md) | 2/4 | 4/4 | 3 high |

**Observation:** the selected version improved credited Worthwhile in both modes, with no measured Fun or hook change. All detailed players followed the muscle project before the tendon project in both conditions. The first two decisions were credited as useful project choices; later compulsory enactments were not. These samples do not demonstrate diverse actual orders or establish that the earlier/later labels invite equal attention to both projects.

**What worked:** players valued the physically different protein destinations and the tendon-to-limb connection. The specific tendon outcome gave the second branch a clearer reason to exist. **What did not improve:** the two messenger → RNA → protein sequences still felt repetitive. Players could follow the supplied source/recipient pictures but did not understand the intracellular mechanism. We retain that bounded explanatory limit rather than adding a specialist cascade to improve an explanation score. This experiment changed setting, names, pictures, goals and specificity together; it cannot isolate which edit caused the score increase. Earlier 3.7/4 Worthwhile evidence used another source version and is not substituted for the fresh matched baseline.

**Decision: keep.** This is a grounded revision with better scoped Worthwhile evidence, not a Fun improvement or proof of student comprehension. The next discriminating change, if needed, would vary the second branch's action pattern while preserving actual tendon biology, rather than adding more messenger terminology. Chapter 5 still has purpose-labelled neural messenger supplies; grounding chapter 3 does not certify those. Its remaining source/identity question stays part of the ongoing anatomy audit.

### Validation and evidence limits

All four campaigns passed original-provider response, independent-grade arithmetic, frozen native replay, model/fresh-thread provenance, audience-guidance and report checks: **12 completed players, 12 grades, four syntheses, 48 actions and 76 accepted fresh tool-free model requests**; no transport retries, protocol rejections or extra look refreshes. Runtime hash `85fa5b68e5cc263b54813f60738100f2d5a4fcc3d19489c4604bff8363b51375`; guidance hash `2f369fe0c06b828b5ff33cf67527e5ef7f570902727c01771b976ccea2400656`. Reports and requested response arrays are retained; temporary candidate, runtime and verification artifacts are discarded.

Native lint has zero errors, the same six mixed-fate warnings and the existing 20,000-state fixed-analysis-limit warning. Native analysis completes the 128-combination reference route; bounded analysis is not exhaustive proof. Chapter 3 was retested in both modes because its information and goal wording changed. Chapters 4–5 received only continuity wording accompanying the renamed shared population, with no changed local actions or goals; their earlier scores remain historical, not newly measured or proof of cumulative effects from this flashback. No whole-story replay study, student test, graphics or screenshots were performed.

<a id="reordered-selected"></a>
## Reordered subjects, reusable muscle cells and whole neurons — 14 November 2026

The user authorized the proposed chapter order and the muscle/neuron corrections. The selected [manuscript](anatomy.md), SHA-256 `c4da372476c51be7476ba165ee05f42622456d24351c11f23b8d51b9b04ed33c`, has **128 combinations on the native reference completion route, 28 playable chapters plus ending 29, 157 recipes and 259 items**. The preceding manuscript was `0510db0384cf39721a3ee619b62509d056087f581a90effb4336c443081ac195`. The praised meal-digestion section is byte-identical, now chapter 18. This is a scoped revision, not completion of the roughly 200-combination ambition or a claim that every chapter now has strong Fun.

### Requirements, hypotheses and changes

- **Muscles (4):** the same existing Muscle cells supply works at either front or back tendon attachments within one fixed arm cutaway. Bone mineralization is a third receiving region. Three physical operations replace five; separate regional cell identities, detached muscle installation and partial-arm intermediates are cut. The later Arm assembly explicitly re-presents that same completed anatomy at model scale. Hypothesis: position explains opposed pulls while a bone/muscle project choice remains. Loss of that choice or belief in different cell types would count against it.
- **Neurons (5):** reuse the established viable population, with appropriate descendant histories disclosed. Two real extracellular messenger inputs develop whole neurons and separate insulating cells. The neuron's own membrane receives channels; the other cells wrap its continuous extension. Either cell type can develop first and either fitting order works. Hypothesis: these independent projects retain choice without detached axons/dendrites, RNA-state padding or another advanced mechanism. Confusion between the two cells' membranes or unexplained specialist prerequisites would count against it.
- **Nutrition (19–20):** remove two absorption journey-results. The completed cutaway shows unchanged molecules crossing; actual Meal glucose then becomes branched carbohydrate inside preserved liver cells. Merge storage with muscle storage and subsequent liver release. The later Glucose is a separate produced sample of the same chemical, not a destination-defined substance. Hypothesis: material versus message is clearer, without hormones supplying glucose atoms or two times being mistaken for one condition.
- **Order:** keep each constructed subject near a meaningful use. Sensory organs follow nerves; airway defenses follow ventilation; immune memory follows lymph; absorption leads directly to storage/release. Skin, injury, repair and renewal lead into final assembly. Hypothesis: the earlier teaching supports these new contexts. Missing prerequisites, weak hooks or unexplained early sensory mechanisms would count against it. Chapter tests cannot settle cumulative pacing.

Two implementation authors worked on bounded muscle/neuron sections. Two **fresh informed reviews**, without prior outcomes, separately inspected the reordered nutrition/assembly arc and the current cell chapters. They were manuscript reviews, not blind predictions. The latter used an isolated tool-free Terra Medium request with only the current excerpt and requirements. Adopted corrections keep blood arrows inside intestinal vessels, show insulin at liver as well as muscle, connect local electrical changes to neighbouring channels, state the purpose of insulating wraps, identify supplied spinal routes, and identify the same lung capillaries at two scales. The reviews do not establish student comprehension. Physiological sources and limits are in the [reading guide](sources/reading-guide.md#whole-cells-and-anatomical-placement); the [curriculum audit](curriculum.md) treats insulating cells as locally taught anatomy, not presumed ordinary-course vocabulary.

### Absorption: failed first fix, then clearer parallel projects

R1 players followed lining → capillaries → vein, then still owed the separately required Blood route intermediate. All three detailed records criticized repeating a connection already pictured. The next hypothesis was to let the completed surface satisfy the blood-route goal as well. **R2** source `77f7fa32028049003ab41906b6203a1a4869886528041718fdc0441173fff15a` removed that duplicate action, but all three detailed players followed the same three-step lining-first path. Fun fell to **0/3**, Worthwhile to **2/3**. This failure is retained, not replaced by the later score. One grader's assertion that no separate blood-route construction was shown conflicts with the initial board's second goal and four visible inputs; that evidence limit does not authorize rewriting its grade.

Players now identified the independent construction choice; the predictable vessel join remained the weakest operation. The preceding applicable detailed evidence is [A1](playtests/2026.11.14_00.43.25/report.md), except the former hormone chapter uses [A2](playtests/2026.11.14_01.10.58/chapter20_report.md) and the finale uses [A6](playtests/2026.11.14_01.25.17/chapter29_report.md). Their source hashes and scopes remain in the preceding selection. The comparison below maps the same subjects into current chapter numbers; the selected column uses R3 for 19 and R1 otherwise. The former one-action liver chapter additionally scored 0/1 Fun and 1/1 Worthwhile; it is now included in 20. Its score is not silently pooled with the four-action hormone chapter. Removing weak operations changes denominators; neither a higher fraction nor fewer credited actions alone proves a gain or loss.

- **Keep the neuron construction:** Fun rose from 1/3 to 2/4, all four actions received Worthwhile credit, and detailed hooks were high. Players distinguished the wrapping cells from the neuron's continuous membrane. Both messenger pairings still felt repetitive, and the full developmental mechanism is not taught. Do not add specialist signalling pathways merely to answer that omitted detail.
- **Keep the direct muscle placement for physical clarity, without claiming a Fun gain:** detailed Fun fell from 2.7/5 to 1.3/3; Worthwhile remained full at 3/3 and hooks were medium. All three valued location-dependent opposed pulls; bone mineralization was the predictable weak action. This is a short application/payoff of chapter 3's muscle-cell and supporting-tissue work, followed by the independently constructed neural example. The user-required shared-cell model is not reversed to recover scores from false regional cell types. Do not pad the chapter with redundant joint/tendon fittings. Its modest choice remains a limit for human review.
- **Keep merged storage/release:** 2.3/5 Fun and 5/5 Worthwhile in detailed play, versus 1/4 and 3/4 for the previous hormone chapter plus the separate liver operation. Players followed glucose material versus hormone messages. One attempted glucose directly at pancreatic cells before understanding the blood/context input; this remains a clarity risk. Fast hooks stayed medium.
- **Keep the subject order provisionally, not as a uniform score improvement:** vision and several local chapters were stable, balance's recorded scores rose, and the spine/eye/ear prerequisites were intact. Immune Worthwhile fell from 3.7/5 to 3.3/5; fast immune Worthwhile remains 2/5. The finale fell from 11/11 to **8.3/11** detailed Worthwhile and from 3.3/11 to **3/11** Fun. Its head/trunk and repaired-skin returns still gave reported payoff, while predictable lower-body fittings remained weak. Fast finale Worthwhile was 10.3/11. This is contrary evidence, not proof the new arc is better; cumulative human pacing remains untested. The finale remains a modest-choice construction payoff from earlier local work, not a high-Fun chapter.

Outstanding item corrections from the human audit remain in **22 (kidney)** and **23 (drinking water)**. Balance's consumed nerve item still led players to seek compatibility information for the other sensor branch. These are recorded unresolved findings, not issues fixed by moving chapters. No screenshot or graphics work was performed.

<a id="cell-and-material-audit"></a>
## Reusable cells and actual substances — human audit, 14 November 2026

The user rejected the chapter-4 split into front/back muscle-cell supplies, the chapter-5 kit of detached cell bodies/branches/long fibres, the slight standalone liver chapter, and the destination-bearing “Nutrients in blood to liver” item. They then requested an audit of chapter 17. These are new human findings against source `0510db0384cf39721a3ee619b62509d056087f581a90effb4336c443081ac195`, not an experience endorsement inherited from its model scores. **At the time of this audit, the manuscript and numbering were unchanged.** The [subsequent applied revision](#reordered-selected) implements the reordering and muscle/neuron/nutrition changes. The kidney and drinking-water findings below remain outstanding, now in chapters 22 and 23. The audit itself did not present a tested replacement specialization chapter; that work belongs to the subsequent revision above.

### Chapter 17: findings and required corrections

| Current representation | Finding | Correction to develop |
|---|---|---|
| Kidney blood sample | A destination-specific copy of Blood already made in chapter 7. Its physical source identity has not changed. | Reuse Blood; show its glucose, salts, water, cells, proteins and wastes in the kidney scene. |
| Blood sample `[transform]` + filter | The output is one separated fraction. Retiring the source suggests all blood became filtered fluid, despite the picture's qualification. | Preserve the Blood source and the physical filter while yielding the filtrate. This does not claim every blood constituent remains quantitatively unchanged. |
| Filtered kidney fluid | A defensible real material: fluid separated across the filter, lacking blood cells and most large proteins, with useful small solutes as well as waste. It is not merely a status badge. | Retain the physical distinction; introduce “filtrate” with a plain gloss such as “filtered fluid.” Keep its pictured composition explicit. |
| Kidney fluid after recovery | An output described by the operation just performed rather than the substance being made. | In this simplified account, finish at Urine, with ordinary downstream processing included and the returned materials shown separately. |
| Urine in the bladder | The same urine in a different location; the extra recipe manufactures a new identity from transport. | Remove this result/recipe. Show flow through the completed drainage anatomy, or use a justified placement interaction retaining Urine's identity. Do not install urine as a permanent organ component. |
| Loose Kidney filter and Kidney recovery tube beside a complete Kidney pair | The intended magnified views are insufficiently distinguished from additional portable parts. | Show fixed, linked close-ups inside the same kidney model. The physical glomerular filter and tubule are receiving structures, not abstract “filtering” and “recovery” hotspots. |

A plausible reduced interaction is `Blood [preserve] @ Kidney filter -> Filtrate`, then `Filtrate [transform] @ Kidney tubule -> Urine`, alongside construction of the ureter/bladder route. The two contexts are actual microscopic structures within the already represented kidneys. This is a proposed authoring form, not a completed integrated chapter or a new scored result. It preserves the worthwhile discovery that useful small substances are filtered and then recovered; it removes the transport-renaming action. [NIDDK, Your Kidneys & How They Work](https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/kidney-disease/kidneys-how-they-work), checked 14 November, grounds the filter/tubule distinction, retained blood cells and large molecules, recovery and urinary drainage. Tubular secretion and the later concentration processes must remain honestly included or qualified rather than claiming that reabsorption alone is the complete physiology.

### Related findings: chapters 14, 15, 18 and 20

“Nutrients in blood to liver” is specifically the earlier glucose, not a new type of nutrient. The same representational problem affects “Amino acids in intestinal blood,” “Water from a drink in blood,” and “Glucose released into blood.” Renaming them while retaining a recipe that merely transports the same substance would not repair the interaction. Keep the substance's identity and make the compartment or physical transformation carry the explanation. Absorption could be shown through the completed intestinal surface without another output tile; any placement-based alternative needs an actual receiving compartment and appropriate prerequisites.

There **is** a natural home for the one-action liver chapter: current chapter 20, Fuel between meals. The player could make a real carbohydrate store and then see its material released, with hormone messages kept distinct from the glucose they regulate. This would make the store useful within one passage and permit deletion of standalone chapter 15. It is a revision hypothesis, not an instruction to add artificial steps or a claim of higher Fun. [OpenStax A&P 24.5](https://openstax.org/books/anatomy-and-physiology-2e/pages/24-5-metabolic-states-of-the-body), checked 14 November, grounds meal-associated storage and between-meal release. Its detailed biochemistry is author background, not a new school-level prerequisite.

### Chapter 4: engine expressivity checked

The actual shared parser, linter and engine were used for a minimal two-target probe. One `Muscle cells` supply was preserved across two rules: at physical front tendon attachments it produced Biceps; at physical back attachments it produced Triceps. The receiving structures transformed into the corresponding fixed muscle results. **Both orders completed, with one shared cells supply and zero semantic lint errors.** An initial probe omitted the required heading icon and failed that format check; the corrected probe passed. No browser, artwork, complete-story carry, student comprehension or Fun claim follows from this check. No temporary probe file was retained.

The receiving picture must represent actual connective tissue/attachments in the arm, with the remaining ordinary tissue and growth conditions supplied, not a conceptual “biceps location” token. Two slots on a single compound accepting the identical Muscle cells item are forbidden by the current format. Separate fixed receiving structures are expressible. Because fixed components cannot be installed into a movable whole, adopting this idiom also requires an explicit plan for the completed arm's later representation, using the previously authorized whole-object handoff if necessary. No engine extension is needed for the two-target distinction itself.

### Chapter 5: distinguish specialization from assembly

A long nerve fibre is the axon of a whole neuron, not an additional cell or a detachable tube supplied for assembly. The current chapter assumes the specialized anatomy it should explain. The reusable starting point is the established unspecialized cell population, drawn with the same membrane, nucleus, mitochondria and ribosomes. Its descendants must become recognizable whole nerve cells, with branches and the long extension growing continuously from the cell. [OpenStax A&P 12.2](https://openstax.org/books/anatomy-and-physiology-2e/pages/12-2-nervous-tissue) grounds those physical relationships; [Biology 16.1](https://openstax.org/books/biology-2e/pages/16-1-regulation-of-gene-expression) grounds different use of existing DNA to produce proteins.

An actual extracellular messenger or explicitly pictured developing tissue could provide a specialization input/context. That is a candidate idiom, not yet an earned choice: relabelling an arbitrary trigger “nerve development” would simply conceal the outcome again. The operation can represent developmental time and included growth materials, with RNA/protein production shown inside the same cells rather than added as draggable cell-state badges. Existing lineage/history must be supplied honestly; no arbitrary mature cell becomes a neuron by receiving a generic chemical. Subsequent channel proteins belong in the continuous cell membrane; loose axons and dendrites should not recur as unexplained starting ingredients. Any additional cell type or insulation operation must earn its inclusion, rather than being added solely to restore a count or a parallel branch.

### Proposed chapter order after the item audit

The user asked whether other chapters should move. This is an editorial dependency review of the current manuscript, **not an applied reorder or a new playtest result**. Numbers below refer to the present manuscript so the ongoing human playthrough remains unambiguous.

| Proposed move | Reason and prerequisite check | Confidence / limit |
|---|---|---|
| Merge 15 (liver storage) into 20 (fuel between meals), and place the combined passage after 14 (absorption). | Glucose, liver storage and subsequent release belong in one material sequence. The earlier muscle, blood and cell machinery are already established. Follow with 16's treatment of the remaining gut contents. | Strong editorial recommendation, contingent on removing journey/status items and retaining actual material roles. Reordering alone does not repair the hormone interactions. |
| Move 21 (airway defenses) immediately after 11 (ventilation). | The existing airway lining and lung air sacs are fresh subjects; their mucus/cell defenses can use those same locations. Blood and its white cells have already been introduced. | Strong adjacency. It introduces the local defense example before the broader immune response. |
| Move 22 (immune memory) immediately after 12 (lymph return). | This places the lymphatic network next to the lymph-node context used for activation and population growth, following the airway defense example. Cell specialization/protein production and white blood cells are earlier. | Strong adjacency; explicitly connect the node to the real lymphatic anatomy. The current chapter-12 network does not mechanically construct the chapter-22 node, and lymph's fluid-return function must retain its own explanation. |
| Move 25–27 (vision, hearing, balance) together after 6 (brain/cord/nerves). | The eyes and ears immediately apply the familiar nerve-cell and brain anatomy. Balance follows both its hearing-coil and eye prerequisites; opposing muscles have been examined in 4. Their explicit recipes do not require later digestive, renal or wound results. | Worth testing, with lower confidence: it lengthens the early nervous-system block and brings the bounded balance mechanism forward. Settle the cell/specialization idiom first. Where these chapters currently supply duplicate nerve-cell views, make reuse explicit rather than assuming a move fixes it. |

Together, these changes produce the candidate sequence **1–6 → 25–27 → 7–11 → 21 → 12 → 22 → 13–14 → (15+20) → 16–19 → 23–24 → 28 → 29 → ending 30**, using original numbers. This keeps the cell introduction intact, retains blood → vessels → heart → lung exchange → ventilation, preserves kidney → water balance, and lets skin → injury → repair → everyday renewal lead into final assembly without the sensory detour. No individual combination within the praised chapter 13 needs changing merely to move its surrounding blocks.

The selection rule is to keep a constructed anatomical subject close to its next meaningful use, while respecting actual operands, physical compartments and audience demand. This order remains a hypothesis: it has not been parsed as a reordered manuscript, mechanically validated or tested for cumulative pacing. Chapter transitions, supplied-item timing, fixed contexts and finale handoffs would need review when applying it. Early completion remains welcome; this is not a proposal to gate otherwise valid recipes.

The [brief's revision checks](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md#lessons-that-govern-every-revision) now own these requirements. At the time of this audit, the preceding selection below was the current tested manuscript. No new fast/detailed campaign was run for the audit itself; the later applied revision and its tests are recorded above.

<a id="local-assembly-revision"></a>
## Local structures, then final assembly — 14 November 2026

The next human playthrough identified a common representational problem across the draft. A ribosome alternated between a loose component and a separate close-up of installed machinery; physiological states acquired their own tiles; normal blood supply became an extra tissue-making step; and many local organs were repeatedly installed into an accumulated body. The user supplied a clearer arc: learn and build individual parts in their own anatomy-textbook chapters, then enjoy putting those parts together in a distinct finale. They explicitly praised chapter 13's food transformations.

The [brief](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md#local-chapters-then-final-assembly) now owns that arc. Its [revision checks](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md#lessons-that-govern-every-revision) require a consistent representational role, a real physical contribution, and a reason for each compound. Preserve valuable distinctions such as opposite muscle actions; remove weak assembly chores. A collection must have actual anatomical organization, boundaries or connections. Whole-body convergence belongs at the end, with independent preparation and flexible order before the justified final covering.

### Feedback, hypotheses and chosen changes

| Hypothesis / human evidence | Revision to test |
|---|---|
| A1 — One object should not alternate between structural part and detached functional view. | Ribosomes must be installed. RNA stays visibly inside the cell, copied from its nucleus; it is not a second draggable ribosome or an external ingredient. Glucose + preserved cell produces ATP; ATP + the same ribosome-containing cell produces protein using supplied amino acids. Five actions, with no working-cell badge. |
| A2 — Cut expendable fittings while preserving worthwhile contrasts. | The elbow joint is already present in living bone. Two regional portions of the earlier muscle-cell population combine with the same supporting tissue to make distinct Biceps and Triceps. Either can attach first. The five-action chapter retains independent bone/muscle projects and the opposite-pulls discovery. |
| A3 — A changed cell shape is still the familiar cell; a general supply requirement is not necessarily a useful combination. | The nerve-cell cutaways reuse the earlier membrane, nucleus and organelle symbols and show long fibres as extensions. Remove the blood-vessel-to-nervous-tissue operation and its intermediate statuses. Keep receiving branches and channel-bearing extensions as independent projects. |
| A4 — A named anatomical connection is clearer than a collection described by its function. | Sensory and motor roots meet in a real spinal nerve. The covering, junction and distinct signal directions are visible. The opening explains that this is one representative spinal nerve among many, without requiring dorsal/ventral or nerve-layer names. |
| A5 — Reuse a substance when a condition changes. | Remove Blood-filled circulation. The completed heart and vessels remain an anatomical assembly; no extra filling operation creates another identity. In chapter 18 reuse Blood from chapter 7; water loss and already-present hormonal signals belong in the scene. Existing completed kidneys become a fixed context there, so that example is not enabled in the earlier normally hydrated construction scene. |
| A6 — A local chapter should end in its own anatomy. | Lymph return, liver storage, colon, urinary organs, skin, clot/repair, eye and ears stay local. Remove their routine whole-body installation steps. The liver becomes a one-action storage payoff after the independent digestion/absorption work, with its low agency explicit. Skin remains the same small outer-forearm sample through repair. |
| A7 — A good human response is a useful design reference. | Preserve chapter 13 byte-for-byte. Its recognizable food substances, independent useful transformations and visible material consequences guide the other revisions. A later model score cannot invalidate this reported human success. |
| A8 — Final integration can have its own independent projects and a genuine convergence. | The initial candidate adds two final playable chapters. First prepare the head, digestive tube and skin covering independently. Then work on the chest and lower body independently, join the regions and head/spine, and add the covering. The initial design used twelve final operations; the selected merged finale retains eleven after the repeated arm fitting is removed, with no completion-token ingredient. The last three joins are an intentional convergence after the preparatory choices. |

The full first candidate was frozen before the final vocabulary pass. A second candidate then replaced the unnecessary orbit/lumen/rectum labels with plain language and made the first arm's supplied hand/shoulder support visible to players. Applying A5 consistently also found the same duplicated-blood problem in chapter 20. **A9:** use the existing Blood with two fixed, physical pancreatic cell populations under clearly shown after-meal/between-meal conditions; name glucagon alongside its liver-release role. This retains two independent branches within four operations without turning the physiological condition into a new blood identity. A fresh chapter-only source reviewer found no biological or prerequisite blocker; their conditional warning about an old literal Muscle fibres identity did not apply—the actual earlier objects are the regional arm muscles, and this chapter explicitly supplies a new microscopic view of that anatomy. It is not claimed to be the same engine item. The ordinary-audience tests must still judge whether that view is clear. The first campaign also found the supplied second-arm fitting repetitive in all six final-chapter trials. **A10:** include that unstudied arm in the supplied chest anatomy, keeping the studied arm's own placement. This cuts the final chapter from eight to seven operations without removing either regional project or changing the three final joins. Focused fast/detailed retests cover chapters 16, 20, 29 and 30 after these edits.

The chapter-4 fresh author compared two variants. The selected five-action variant presents front/back populations as portions of the already made muscle cells, then builds both muscles at equal detail. This is an explicitly supplied regional partition, not a one-to-two split operation; the shared supporting tissue remains a literal operand. The rejected six-action variant retained a common muscle-bundle input but needed two further tendon/connective-tissue ingredients, recreating the part catalogue the user rejected.

The finale's fresh author compared a single torso accepting all organs with a regional preparation-and-join arc. The latter was selected because the head/gut/skin and chest/lower-body projects offer stronger alternatives than fitting everything onto one base in different orders. Unnamed late slots on the existing neural and breathing assemblies avoid advertising empty eye/arm slots in earlier text-only chapters; the final goals name those fittings. Useful precocious assembly remains legal where components already exist. Chapter order guides attention rather than adding artificial recipe gates.

### Source review, scopes and limits

A fresh reviewer inspected only the first six current chapters, the applicable audience/physical-item requirements and format semantics. They found no blocking issue in the cell, muscle or nerve-cell treatments, but caught the unannounced expansion from one spinal nerve to a whole network. The opening now identifies the nerve as one representative example. A second fresh reviewer inspected the current local-organ/finale manuscript, without old scores or working notes. They found no blocking biology or prerequisite defect and requested two corrections: remove a stale circulation-connection instruction from the now-local liver chapter, and retain the already examined liver/pancreas cells when describing their larger-scale final organs. Both were corrected before playtesting.

The root-to-spinal-nerve account is grounded in [OpenStax 13.4](https://openstax.org/books/anatomy-and-physiology-2e/pages/13-4-the-peripheral-nervous-system). The final handoffs retain the same anatomical structures, but the engine does not enforce shared identity for every changed-scale view. `final-ears` is a new primitive representing the completed hearing/balance anatomy once; `final-urinary-organs` represents the already completed kidney pair, ureters and bladder, whose earlier view is fixed in the water chapter. These use the user's expressly allowed complete-compound-to-later-whole presentation handoff. Required preceding chapters establish the represented construction; the source and future artwork establish continuity. Do not claim the engine proves that narrative equivalence.

The final model supplies unstudied tissues explicitly: the other arm, lower body's normal pelvis/legs/walls, surrounding skin, facial support and second eye, and unexamined upper-digestive anatomy. It does not claim the player has constructed every human structure from molecules. The studied skin segment stays small and occupies one matching inspection opening in the supplied covering; it does not grow into all skin or undergo a new graft. Only the later complete ear view is fitted; the earlier hearing coil is not installed a second time. The lymph network already has its vein junction and is placed at that existing junction, not given another one.

The user's Chapter 12 observation remains an **unresolved visual question**. A label or text picture does not establish that a regional vascular model looks like one comprehensible thing. Localizing the chapter and postponing its placement addresses the narrative premise; actual graphical unity, scale and readability await artwork. No graphics or screenshot testing is part of this revision.

### Testing the finale structure

The first two-chapter design and its shorter follow-up still gave predictable fittings and modest hooks. A fresh structural reviewer saw only the current finale, the brief and the standing criteria, with no scores or earlier proposals. They compared a single eleven-action assembly with the two-chapter layout. They recommended a third arrangement: move the skin-patch fitting from the first chapter into the second, adding an independent skin project beside chest and lower body. Their concern about the full merge was roughly twelve starting objects versus about nine in the alternative climax. The same known organs might still be easier to understand than a crowded board of unfamiliar tissue pieces, so both layouts were tested.

**A11:** compare the full merge (A4) against the skin-transfer layout (A5), keeping the eleven operations and physical subjects. The full merge exposed an avoidable prerequisite problem: every detailed player tried joining the chest and lower body before completing the arm and lymph fittings. **A12:** explicitly name all regional fittings in the initial goal and say to finish each region before joining it; retest this merged candidate as A6. No failed attempt is treated as desirable challenge. The regional ordering is an anatomical-model assembly convention, not a claimed biological necessity.

Before these structural tests, an unscored A3 correction removed a newly added unconditional chest picture: item readings are global, so A2 could show completed chest anatomy prematurely. The corrected version describes the supplied opposite arm only with the final arm fitting, and restores the breathing chapter's default/diaphragm/lung readings exactly to tested A1. A3 itself was not a scored cohort; both tested structural variants include the correction. The selected local chapter-16/20 observations retain A2 checkpoint-history limits: their own text and operations are unchanged, while the unrelated premature chest picture is removed from earlier history. Two stale author-only chapter references were also corrected.

### Why the retained compounds exist

| Compound | Subsequent purpose |
|---|---|
| Cell | Its fitted structures enable ATP and protein production without replacing the cell. |
| Completed kidney pair | Collects the filtered remainder through ureters into the bladder, then demonstrates water conservation; a later same-anatomy handoff places the completed urinary organs. |
| Skin segment | Joins actual skin layers, sweat outlet and sensory endings; the local injury/repair sequence uses that region. |
| Skin with a cleared wound | Receives newly grown tissue and a connected vessel before the temporary clot is cleared. |
| Existing neural/head assembly | Keeps skull, brain, cord and spine while the eye/ears meet their brain connections; the completed region joins the trunk. |
| Existing breathing/chest assembly | Retains lungs, heart, chest muscles and diaphragm while arms and the lymph route meet their actual regional connections. |
| Lower body | Provides real pelvic/leg/wall anatomy around the distinct digestive and urinary routes before the regions join. |
| Skin covering | Incorporates the original small sample and then closes the anatomical model's assembly openings. |

A compound's existence is not justified solely because the format can encode it. These are source-level reasons to retain them; playtests and, later, artwork must still establish that they read clearly and are worth doing.

### Selected manuscript and measured evidence

The selected manuscript is **A6**, SHA-256 `0510db0384cf39721a3ee619b62509d056087f581a90effb4336c443081ac195`: **131 combinations on the native reference completion route, 29 playable chapters plus a text ending, 164 recipes and 266 items**. The finale contributes eleven operations across one chapter. Alternatives and player attempts can change route/action counts. Native lint reports **zero errors and six reviewed mixed-fate warnings**, with no fixed-analysis-limit warning. These concern implicit fittings retaining their parent and later whole transformations: Skin segment, the cleared wound, Lower body, Breathing apparatus, the neural/head assembly and Skin covering. Native analysis reaches the ending.

This is a revision of physical representation and narrative structure, **not a claim that every chapter now has high Fun or hook**. The approximately 200-combination aspiration remains open; this version does not regain length by restoring the rejected body-installation steps.

### What the observations support

- **A1, consistent installed machinery: supported with a hook reservation.** Chapter 2 moves from 1.7/6 to 2.7/5 detailed Fun and retains five credited Worthwhile actions. All three valued ATP-supported protein production in the same cell. Detailed hooks nevertheless fall from 2H/1M to 3M; mitochondria placement remains predictable. Clearer physical roles are not proof of stronger curiosity.
- **A2, preserve the muscle contrast: supported.** Chapter 4 reaches 2.7/5 Fun and 5/5 Worthwhile, with 1H/2M detailed hooks versus the preceding 2/4, 4/4 and 2H/1M. All three singled out opposite pulls as the worthwhile payoff. Making the second muscle repeats the first construction, but the resulting distinction matters. The separate elbow fitting stays cut.
- **A3/A4, physical nerve anatomy: mixed.** The shortened nerve-cell chapter has 1/3 Fun versus 2/4 previously, retaining 3/3 Worthwhile and 2H/1M. Removing a blood-supply operation does not itself improve choice. The next chapter's real spinal-nerve connection has 3/5 Fun, 5/5 Worthwhile and 2H/1M. The channel explanation remains a bounded advanced topic, not a reason to add membrane machinery.
- **A5, stable Blood identity: clearer representation, limited measured change.** Chapter 18 remains 1/2 Fun and 2/2 Worthwhile with 3M detailed hooks. Chapter 9's heart-and-vessel anatomy retains strong construction results without the filling-status tile. These results do not require bringing back water-state or blood-filled-body identities.
- **A6, local endpoints: structural requirement met; score gains are not general.** Lymph falls from 4.3/7 Fun to 1/3 when the regional integration work is removed; its three actions are all credited Worthwhile, but its hook is 3M. Kidney falls from 5/6 to 3/5 detailed Worthwhile and retains predictable drainage fittings. Skin keeps 3.3 meaningful actions with one fewer required fitting; eye keeps 2 meaningful actions and credits all four remaining actions as Worthwhile. Repair improves Fun 4/8 → 4.3/8 while Worthwhile falls 8/8 → 7.3/8. No rejected whole-body installation is restored to recover a count.
- **A7, the successful digestion chapter: preserved.** Its chapter text is byte-identical to the user's praised version. Current detailed Fun is still 3/5, Worthwhile 4.7/5 and hooks 1H/2M. Models now credit fewer Worthwhile actions than the preceding 5/5; the text did not change, so this is not evidence of a damaging edit.

- **A9, consistent pancreatic contexts: role clarification, no Fun gain.** A2 retains 3/4 detailed Worthwhile and 1H/2M hooks, but Fun falls 2/4 → 1/4 and fast Worthwhile falls 3.3/4 → 3/4. The two releases are repetitive; the muscle-storage/liver-release consequences remain valued. Keep the ordinary Blood identity and visible conditions, without calling this a successful Fun improvement or adding more hormonal machinery.
- **Plain vocabulary in 16/29: mixed.** Colon detailed Worthwhile rises 3.3/4 → 4/4 and Fun 1.3/4 → 2/4, while fast Worthwhile falls 2.7/4 → 2.3/4. The first assembly chapter's detailed Worthwhile falls 4/4 → 3/4 and introduces a Low hook despite plainer wording. Small independent samples do not support a causal claim that removing specialist nouns improves or harms enjoyment.
- **A10, cut the repeated second arm: the specific repetition is removed, but the overall improvement hypothesis fails.** A2's last chapter falls from 1.7/8 to 1.3/7 Fun, 6.3/8 to 4.3/7 detailed Worthwhile, and 7/8 to 6/7 fast Worthwhile; two fast hooks become Low. Keep the explicitly supplied arm in the model, but do not treat the cut as sufficient. These results prompted the structural alternatives below.

### Finale structure experiment

| Variant and passage | Detailed Fun | Detailed Worthwhile | Fast Worthwhile | Hooks: detailed; fast |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A4, chapter 29 | 4/12 | 7.3/12 | 9.3/11 | 3M; 1M/2L |
| A5, chapter 29 | 1/3 | 2.7/3 | 2.3/3 | 3M; 3M |
| A5, chapter 30 | 2.3/8 | 7.3/8 | 8/8 | 3M; 2M/1L |
| A6, chapter 29 | 3.3/11 | 11/11 | 10/11 | 2M/1L; 3M |

**A11/A12 verdict and selection: A6, one final assembly chapter.** A4's merged board did not by itself establish more enjoyable choice: all three detailed players made the same premature regional-join attempt. A6's explicit preparation goal removes those attempts, while detailed Worthwhile rises 7.3/12 → 11/11 and fast Worthwhile 9.3/11 → 10/11. Detailed Fun falls 4/12 → 3.3/11; the removed failure is not something to recover. Hooks improve from 2L/1M to 3M in fast play, but worsen from 3M to 1L/2M in detailed play.

A5 is a credible smaller-board alternative: its two passages scored 2.7/3 and 7.3/8 detailed Worthwhile, with 1/3 and 2.3/8 Fun; detailed hooks were all Medium, while the last fast chapter had one Low. These are separate chapter cohorts, not one continuous eleven-action trial. The merged A6 does **not** establish a Fun improvement over those counts. It is selected for the single uninterrupted assembly of the known parts, simultaneous regional availability, no repeated premature joins, and its stronger detailed construction payoff. That trades away the smaller board and does not establish universally stronger hooks. The roughly twelve-object board remains a finished-art readability question.

A2 is `7797a7a211dd0e0a03148d0c7d7719dff4ce6cc8b9d3157a732aff3ced6a1968`; A4 is `83f65f8805d7f6e0ca300ef53ae3a24df21daec9d4df49953b2555f6b7dcea4e`; A5 is `1d7d081d5acd464dd7ba7557b15a4912d1744caea2e6b43d61d2c7a29c19e4cf`. A6's exact selected hash is above. No discarded playable manuscript remains alongside the canonical story.

### Payoffs and remaining weaknesses

The one-action liver storage chapter is explicitly **0/1 Fun, 1/1 Worthwhile, 3M hook**. It is a brief consequence of the independently constructed digestion and absorption projects immediately before it, not preparation excused as payoff. All three valued the physical store; its earlier whole-body addition is gone. The immune chapter remains a provisional consequence of differentiation/protein building and the preceding airway defenses: 1.3/5 detailed Fun, 3.7/5 Worthwhile, 2H/1M, but only 2/5 fast Worthwhile. That disagreement still prevents calling it an established high-worth payoff.

The final three joins are intentional convergence after independent regional projects in the final assembly phase. Players consistently valued the chest/abdomen boundary and retained final cutaway. Their predictable pairings are reported as low decision value; no score is increased because they are intended as payoff. Checkpoint players did not personally make all the earlier parts, so the emotional effect of a continuous playthrough remains untested.

Other weaknesses remain visible: blood's final mixing repeats; the diaphragm fitting is obvious; the absorption chapter shows the liver route before separately asking players to construct it; kidney drainage repeats named slots; hearing and balance have some prerequisite/consumed-route uncertainty; renewal's skin branch is predictable. These are recorded findings, not claims that every passage meets the full ambition. The opening remains short with one independent choice in three actions; it is not designated a payoff. The four advanced topics have current evidence above, with especially modest lymph/balance hooks; they receive no blanket endorsement from a syllabus heading.
