Only the unchanged comparison has the separately checked identical prior context and global definitions. Local links in the full-story documents and the newly added dated note resolve. Older unrelated note links were outside this check. Tracked changes and the new manuscript/study pass whitespace checks.

The requested structured player-response arrays, chapter reports and mechanically assembled campaign reports remain under `playtests/`. Disposable source freezes, review packets, author variants, raw model output, intermediate grades, custom orchestration and runtime sessions are removed after recording these outcomes. These retained records are selected evidence, not complete replay bundles. The licensed local source library remains ignored research for the active development project and can be deleted when development ends. The project remains active; this full-story draft and evaluation pass do not constitute publication or completion of artwork and student testing.

## Narrative arc


### Current cell-first textbook arc

The [full manuscript](anatomy.md) has **32 chapters and 172 required combinations** after the [enclosed-cell correction](#enclosure-selected), following the [ordinary-biology revision](#ordinary-selected). The [design brief](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md) owns lasting intent; the [curriculum audit](curriculum.md) separates ordinary-course relationships, locally taught anatomy and four bounded excursions. Earlier studies preserve their original versions and scores.

| Passage | Anatomical/physiological question | Grounded reason to care |
|---|---|---|
| 1–2: boundary and energy | How can a cell exchange material yet maintain a different inside? | One compound encloses fluid, nucleus, transport proteins and organelles. Different molecules use different routes; ATP supports both membrane work and protein production. Glucose entry and respiration stay together. |
| 3–4: division and specialization | How do related cells become numerous and different? | DNA copying preserves instructions; different gene use changes proteins and cell capabilities. Cells also make material outside themselves. |
| 5–6: bone, joints and muscle | How do living cells and supporting material make useful structures? | Mineral and fibres carry loads; surfaces and sleeves permit motion; opposite muscle attachments turn pulling into different movements. |
| 7–8: nerve cells and nervous system | How does information cross between cells and travel around a body? | Receiving branches, sending fibres and chemical messages have different roles; organized central structures coordinate peripheral routes. |
| 9–13: blood, vessels, heart, lungs and breathing | What makes transport and exchange possible? | Blood is a working material; wall thickness, valves, double circulation, thin interfaces and changing chest volume do different jobs. The heart itself needs blood. |
| 14: integration and lymph | How do separately built systems meet? | Nerves carry signals, blood delivers materials and lymph returns escaped tissue fluid. Movement needs all of them and cell-level energy. |
| 15–20: food, liver, colon, kidneys and water | What reaches cells, and what should stay or leave? | Digestion differs from absorption and storage; microbial inhabitants participate; filtration requires recovery; water conservation does not replace drinking. |
| 21–23: skin, heat and fuel | How can an exchanging body maintain its conditions? | A water-resistant boundary can still release sweat; responses to warming/cooling and feeding/fasting use different existing structures. |
| 24–25: defense and immune memory | How do physical defenses and living cells respond to microbes? | Mucus clearance differs from engulfment; matching recognition can generate antibody-producing and memory descendants. Present clearance and a later response are different outcomes. |
| 26–27: clot and repair | Does stopping a leak restore tissue? | A plug, reinforcing mesh, surface closure, blood supply and lasting support are separate achievements. Earlier division/protein production matter again. |
| 28–31: senses and timing | How do physical events become information and coordinated responses? | Focusing light differs from sensing it; vibration and movement use mechanical sensors; a body's ongoing rhythm can be adjusted by light. |
| 32: renewal | What persists while a body's material changes? | Iron from worn red cells can be reused, and skin replaces itself. Continuing identity depends on ongoing work, not unchanging parts. |

The arc is a textbook reconstruction across scales, not manufacture of a living person or a complete developmental timeline. It retains short payoffs after independent projects but does not excuse ordinary weak assembly chapters. The final score table records uneven Fun and mostly Medium hooks. The approximately 200-action/20–30-chapter goal remains an aspiration; rejected merges and removed fittings should not return simply to hit it.

### Earlier ten-chapter exploration (historical)


This earlier comparison tested two arrangements under the [same brief](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md): *From the inside out*, the cells-first experiment, and *A hand that keeps itself*, the arm-first reference, following the four-chapter *Lift a glass* prototype. Their manuscripts have been removed. The following account records the earlier designs; the sole current story is [anatomy.md](anatomy.md), described above.

The cells-first opening follows **cellular ingredients → working muscle cells → organized muscles → an articulated arm**. Its first three chapters have eight, five and six combinations. Fuel enters the already constructed cells, so their internal work remains attached to the thing the player made. The fitted cell becomes one item that can participate in tissue construction; the tissue's organization then becomes essential to moving the joint. This order tests the pleasure of building a body from smaller beginnings.

The arm-first arrangement instead begins with the question of what will make a movable framework work. It has 62 combinations. The current cells-first version also has a 62-action completion route, with 82 rules including generated compound fittings and alternative orders. Its local constructions, compounds and repair production have changed substantially; the comparison is not an order-only experiment. Both are representative ten-chapter passages, not commitments to the eventual full game's boundaries. Their later organ assemblies can be developed at smaller scales in the longer story.

The table describes the preferred cells-first ordering. The arm-first comparison asks about the framework first, then explores the cells and attaches their organized pulls. Normal compatible-partner guidance remains part of play.

| Chapter | Construction and changing question | Grounded worthwhile proposition | Principal risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Inside the muscle | A specific muscle-cell model receives its organelles and contractile structures. | A cell's boundary, instructions, protein building and energy machinery have different jobs. A completed compound becomes a usable whole through the addition of real force-producing structures. | An organelle inventory; excessive setup before meaningful use. |
| 2. A pull has a running cost | Fuel use → ATP-supported fibers → organized muscle bundles → opposing pulls. | Work requires a continuing energy process, and tissue organization transmits cell-level tension to bones. | Avoid making the supported fibers seem permanently powered; ATP renewal requires continuing supplies. |
| 3. A joint is not a motor | Living bone frameworks, mineral, cartilage, joint fluid and ligaments → a framework receiving the previously built muscles. How will attachment geometry use their pulls? | Composite materials and interfaces carry loads; a structure can permit movement without powering it. | Simple preparations may be weaker than seeing the earlier muscles become useful. |
| 4. Feeling the movement | Motor fibers cooperate with insulating support cells; incoming sight and position information joins outgoing control. | Different cells cooperate within a nerve; a request for work differs from its supplies, and feedback guides an action while it happens. | Signal routes becoming a predictable catalogue. |
| 5. Getting there and back | Squeezing heart chambers initially move fluid both ways; valves direct the flow, and pacemaker cells establish rhythm. A vessel circuit receives pump and blood in either order. Red cells differ from muscle cells. | Pump, transport medium, exchange and return are different necessities. | Heart parts that add labels without a useful distinction; several explanations arriving on the last fitting. |
| 6. Air and blood stay separate | Airways, sacs and thin exchange layers meet blood flow and chest-driven ventilation in either order. A flowing circuit without renewed air exposes a different limitation from ventilation without blood flow. | Crossing an interface differs from moving along a route; oxygen reaches distant cells without air entering their vessels. | Explicitly named fittings can remain a checklist even when the results are valued. |
| 7. Food has not arrived yet | Muscle layers first surround the gut; adding a meal makes mixing and movement visible. Enzymes change food molecules before lining and blood routes permit absorption and distribution. | Muscle can move the contents of a tube as well as pull a bone. Changing food chemically, mixing it and absorbing it are different contributions. | A linear mechanism sequence still needs results that reward attention. |
| 8. A living border | Progressive cell sheets, outer covering and deeper support form skin; support can join before or after the outer covering, and skin before or after the arm's supply. The hand lifts and holds the glass. | Surface cells receive supplies from vessels in the layer beneath them; living cells maintain a useful outer layer that is no longer living. Holding still also requires continuing work. | A routine final join; one route bypasses the outer-layer wonder, so its picture must carry the distinction. |
| 9. Closed is not rebuilt | A small accidental scrape affects this body's skin; local sealing and cleanup follow. | Limiting loss is different from replacing tissue. Local cooperation does not wait for conscious instructions. | Scripted wound states or an unnecessary manufactured emergency. |
| 10. The work that stays invisible | Instructions and material yield protein within a persistent resident repair cell; its machinery exports support, and surface cells restore coverage. | Cells make structures beyond their own boundaries. Movement and repair both require ongoing cellular work, and visible stillness does not mean rest at every scale. | Players infer processing but miss its required cell context; a higher decision score can include this interface friction. |

### Why the scale changes might earn their time

The sequence tries to connect four different explanations: geometry determines the direction of a pull; cellular work supplies tension and material; circulation supplies distant tissues; local responses restore a boundary after damage. The return to cells is intended to make the first cell chapter matter twice, without turning those muscle cells into skin cells. The skin's own residents perform the later repair.

No dissection hotspot is included. The repair cell is a fixed producer context, not a place to take the body apart. The current one-result format would make unpacking a prepared kit easy to represent but would not by itself explain why taking it apart was worthwhile. Injury tests the role of a previously built boundary more directly. No medical treatment is taught.

The first lift and hold now close chapter eight, before the injury. The repair finale returns to a body whose ordinary action has already been demonstrated. Healing does not unlock the first possibility of movement. Repair is an additional account of what keeps a body useful, rather than a prerequisite imposed on every ordinary reach.

### Evidence sought

The current revision uses planned medical-textbook-style illustrations and item titles for most of the explanation, purposeful goals, two-to-four-sentence openings and sparse short wonders. Full scientific grounding remains in author notes. The [expansion study](#expansion-three-further-fast-revision-rounds) records earlier Terra `--fast` trials. The subsequent [detailed study](#ten-chapter-detailed-trials) compares meaningful decisions, worthwhile results and hook over the earlier baseline and three rounds plus five further rounds, including rejected changes and weak combination patterns. The [full-story study](#full-story-development) now develops the separately authorized roughly 200-action story; this table describes the retained ten-chapter reference.

The test is whether a player can explain a relationship from the constructions and depicted changes, before or without an accompanying wonder. Text agents reading `{...}` illustration plans supply provisional evidence about that representation, not evidence of recognizing rendered art or learning as a student. Compatibility remains supplied. The studies test local chapter value and reported curiosity; no score establishes student learning or endurance across 200 combinations.

## Starting ideas and provenance


### The user's direction

The worthwhile question is what we learn about the world and humans' place in it. It is not enough that the real-world activity being represented would be worthwhile. Anatomy and physiology offer a promising subject because familiar human acts depend on unfamiliar relationships within us.

**Undissection** reverses dissection: bring separate parts into relationships until a functioning body emerges. The user suggested both many parts converging into one and a few basic things first proliferating into more complex things before converging. Preserve the satisfaction of construction found in Settlement and Creation without assuming that copying their progression guarantees their appeal.

Homeostasis can supply concrete purposes, such as regulating temperature. Structural assembly should reveal physiology: circulation delivers oxygen and nutrients; metabolism produces wastes and heat; removal and regulatory feedback let the system continue. A final whole should do something, rather than merely have all its labels attached.

The user favours a modern account but is open to historical approaches (humours, Harvey, Bernard/Cannon), different animals such as frogs and pigs, and precedents in medical teaching. The intended players are grades 10–12 students who have studied biology. The user's anecdote about failing anatomy and physiology despite otherwise excellent academic results establishes personal interest, not evidence of any particular learning deficit.

### Possibilities worth comparing

1. **Few → many → one.** Reuse a small vocabulary of living tissues to build structures, connect structures into systems, then let the systems sustain one body.
2. **Make an inside.** Boundaries, selective exchange and an internal fluid environment explain why merely swallowing food or filling lungs does not deliver anything to distant cells.
3. **A body that answers back.** Assemble circulation, ventilation, heat exchange and sensors; a change in surroundings reveals the difference between a working mechanism and a regulated one.
4. **Four tissues, many jobs.** Epithelial, connective, muscle and nervous tissues recur in different proportions and arrangements. Avoid treating them as interchangeable Lego or complete two-ingredient organ recipes.
5. **An ordinary minute.** Standing, walking, drinking or speaking brings previously separate systems into one recognizable act. The ordinary act must earn the preparatory work.
6. **Relatives.** Compare homologous structures or contrasting physiological solutions in other animals. Evolution explains shared plans and compromises; it is not a ladder culminating in humans.
7. **The first breath.** Placental exchange gives way to lungs and altered circulation at birth. Potentially powerful, but a developmental story with substantial factual demands rather than generic organ assembly.
8. **Changing explanations.** Follow historical investigators from apparently independent parts to circulation and internal regulation. Do not turn incorrect historical models into factual recipes.

### Precedents to investigate

- Silverthorn, *Human Physiology: An Integrated Approach*: common principles, then systems, then explicit integration.
- Sherwood, *Human Physiology: From Cells to Systems*: recurrent homeostatic purpose and each system's contribution to the whole.
- Zahourek, *Anatomy in Clay*: constructive anatomy combining muscles, vessels and nerves in their spatial relationships.
- OpenStax anatomy/physiology: an accessible factual reference, subject to checking the licence of the particular edition used.
- Spongelab *Build-a-Body*: related assembly premise. Availability and actual interaction remain to be checked; do not claim novelty or personal play experience from a description.

The [source library](sources/README.md) separates downloaded readings, publisher previews, original study notes and unavailable material.

### Lessons carried into this study

- The project's [philosophy](../../README.md#philosophy) asks for understanding worth carrying beyond play. The [authoring guide](../../docs/story-authoring.md) supplies the adopted practice.
- A valuable finished object does not make every digital fitting valuable. The Contraptions failure is a reason to test a complete small arc before expanding anatomy coverage.
- Recognizable subjects, meaningful material relationships and consequential discoveries have stronger precedent than invented tokens whose significance exists only in explanatory prose.
- High AI scores do not overrule a weak human premise. Chapter playtests cannot establish whole-story attachment or human enjoyment.
- No graphics in this phase. If a step depends on a picture of a precise spatial fitting, it cannot earn a text-play success by asking the reader to imagine that picture.

## Early brainstorms


These are design candidates, not assertions of tested enjoyment or physiological recipes. Each comparison gives its reviewers the same audience, interaction format and worthwhile criterion.

### Round 1: what is being reconstructed?

#### A — Four tissues, one body

Start with epithelial, connective, muscle and nervous tissue as reusable families. Arrange them into a protective surface, an exchange surface, a pump, flexible tubes and a moving limb. Intermediate structures proliferate. Fit the exchange surfaces to the circulation; join sensing and muscles to coordinated movement; converge the structures into one person who can take a walk.

**Combination angle:** the same material performs different jobs depending on its arrangement and partners. A thin surface joined to a capillary bed enables exchange; contractile tissue around a chamber enables pumping. Intermediate recipes would represent assemblies with explicitly provided support, not magical two-tissue organ growth.

**Worthwhile angle:** the body is not a bag of unrelated organs. Much of its variety comes from reusing a small repertoire in different relationships. A familiar action draws on structures the player made separately.

**Risk:** four jargon-like tiles might become universal ingredients; the player could spend the game naming familiar organs rather than discovering physiology. The walk could merely certify completion.

#### B — Make an inside

Begin with a patch of living cells, a selective boundary, fluid and a supply of nutrients. The first enclosed space protects an interior, but its cells now need deliveries and waste removal. Build circulation, connect an air-exchange surface and a gut, then assemble kidney filtration and recovery. Finally connect sensing to the mechanisms that adjust flow, water retention and heat loss. The construction becomes one body that keeps a livable interior while interacting with the outside.

**Combination angle:** solving one problem exposes another. A boundary separates; transport crosses it; a circuit serves distant cells; filtration removes too much until recovery makes it selective. Connections consume separate model modules into a growing whole.

**Worthwhile angle:** being alive depends on maintaining conditions around cells. Eating, breathing, sweating and urinating are related exchanges across a boundary. The gut's contents are not yet inside that protected environment.

**Risk:** the early cell-to-body transition can imply a false evolutionary or developmental sequence. “Stable interior” is abstract unless the player sees concrete consequences. Regulatory arrows can turn into paperwork tokens.

#### C — One ordinary minute

Start with support, contraction, sensing and exchange modules. Construct an arm that bends, a route from lungs to working muscle and a way to coordinate movement. Ask the body to stand and walk. Increased demand then gives the player a reason to connect circulation, ventilation, temperature regulation and water conservation. End with a person drinking a glass of water after a short walk, the same systems responding together.

**Combination angle:** build capabilities and apply them to familiar circumstances; the same circulation supports limb, lung, kidney and skin. The growing body becomes the workpiece on which later relationships operate.

**Worthwhile angle:** effortless-seeming acts depend on continuous work within us. An ordinary minute becomes more intelligible, without manufacturing a crisis or medical diagnosis.

**Risk:** goals could tell the player every answer, while each organ arrives exactly when needed. The everyday ending may be too weak to justify a long sequence; challenge-response steps may rename an already functioning body.

### Round 2: where does the player discover a relationship?

All three candidates below reconstruct a simplified physiological model; assembly order is neither embryology nor evolution. They use ordinary compatible-pair hints, no timers or simulated resource quantities. The stated results must carry the consequences in text. These are operation sketches, not final executable recipes.

#### D — Three crossings

Begin with blood, a heart, vessels and exchange surfaces. Assemble a circuit, then branch its interfaces toward air, food and waste. Air enters lungs but oxygen must cross into moving blood. Food enters a gut but absorbed molecules must cross into the internal environment. Kidney filtration sends useful substances out with wastes; tubules return much of what the body needs. Connect the interfaces into one body, then follow a drink of water through the system.

Representative work: heart + blood vessels → circuit; circuit + blood → circulation; lung air sacs + pulmonary capillaries → gas-exchange interface; gut lining + intestinal capillaries → absorption interface; kidney filter + recovery tubule → nephron. Specialized interfaces join the continuing circulation rather than creating independent bodies. Each module includes its ordinary supporting anatomy, described concretely.

**Combination angle:** one circulation joins three distinct boundary crossings; output reveals which substances move where. **Worthwhile angle:** the body's outline is not the boundary that matters to its cells. **Risk:** modules supplied ready-made could make the opening small set misleading. Three rounds of “interface + circulation” might become the same action. A drink at the end could be a weak demonstration unless it changes a previously constructed state.

#### E — Save the useful things

Start with a blood supply, a pressure-driven capillary filter, a tubule and transport cells. Build a kidney model that initially filters small molecules indiscriminately, then recovers glucose, salts and water into blood. Join its output to a complete circulation/respiration model, add a sensor-to-hormone-to-tubule relationship, and contrast ordinary conditions with water lost through sweating. End with the body conserving water while still excreting wastes.

Representative work: blood supply + glomerular filter → filtrate in a nephron; filtrate-bearing tubule + recovery transport → selective return; water-loss condition + body model → concentrated-body-fluid state; sensed concentration + hormone pathway → increased water recovery. The final states are observations of the same model, not new organs. Supporting kidney gradients and energy supply would be provided explicitly.

**Combination angle:** reverse an apparently sensible first solution—discard everything small—by adding selective return and adjustable recovery. **Worthwhile angle:** human survival depends on continuous discrimination, not simply eliminating “bad things”; water conservation connects kidneys to skin. **Risk:** starting with kidney details may be too technical and late body assembly may feel bolted on. A hormone represented as a tool could disguise a substantial causal chain. This is a kidney story unless wider integration earns its cost.

#### F — Build, then meet the weather

Start with a pump, blood, vessels and a thin exchange surface. Build oxygen delivery and nutrient absorption, then a kidney and a skin surface. Converge them into a resting body model. Connect temperature sensing to a control centre and from it to sweat glands and skin vessels. A warm afternoon increases heat gain; the completed connections cause heat loss but also water loss. Connect concentration sensing and antidiuretic hormone to kidney water recovery. End with drinking and recovery rather than an invulnerable “finished” body.

Representative work: ventilated lung surface + circulation → oxygen-delivering circuit; intestinal absorption + circuit → fed tissues; kidney recovery + circulation → recovered solutes; temperature sensor + controller → temperature feedback; feedback + skin effectors → heat-loss response; sweating body + dry conditions → water deficit; kidney regulation + water deficit → water-conserving body.

**Combination angle:** constructions later act together under a specific condition, and one useful response changes what another system must do. **Worthwhile angle:** stability is ongoing work with costs and limits; the environment remains essential. **Risk:** arbitrary environmental-condition tiles could become test buttons. “Fed tissues” and “water-conserving body” must change what can happen next, rather than merely award labels. The feedback stages could repeat an obvious wiring gesture.

### Round 3: three complete prototype scopes

The setting is an explanatory reconstruction of a living body, not surgery or development. Binary combinations connect structures or apply an external condition to the assembled model. Results are authored text, with no live fluid simulation. The intended audience has studied biology. A few → many → one progression is desirable; it does not justify false two-ingredient organ recipes. All candidates end with one integrated body model, not a claim to have fabricated a person.

#### G — A glass of water

Three or four short chapters, roughly 12–18 combinations. Begin with blood supply, a capillary filter, a nephron tubule and selective transport. Construct filtration, then recovery: useful glucose appears in filtrate before transport returns it to blood. Construct water recovery from a concentrated kidney medulla and a collecting duct whose permeability can change; connect it to the earlier nephron. Assemble concentration sensing, the hypothalamus/posterior-pituitary ADH pathway and the collecting duct response. Bring gut absorption, skin water loss and the circulating tissues into one body model. Apply substantial sweating without drinking: urine becomes more concentrated but the body remains short of water. Absorb a drink, then use the restored condition to reduce the conservation signal. The final achievement is continuing exchange and adjustment.

**Combination angle:** a small collection branches into filtering, recovering and regulating structures, then joins the body; later inputs expose what those constructions can and cannot accomplish. **Worthwhile angle:** much kidney work is returning useful things; compensation cannot replace a missing supply. Sweat, urine and thirst concern the same internal water. **Hook:** the apparently wasteful filter, the cost of successful cooling, then the limits of conservation. **Risk:** this is a complete physiological episode, not complete anatomy. ADH and medullary gradients could bury the accessible insight; the body may arrive too nearly complete.

#### H — The cost of cooling

Four short chapters, roughly 16–22 combinations. Build a thin exchange bed and a thick protective surface, then connect the protective surface to blood vessels, sweat glands and temperature sensing. Assemble lung and intestinal exchange around a common circulation. Join the structures into a body that can cool through evaporation. A warm walk with sweat loss exceeding intake reveals a water deficit. Assemble kidney filtration, selective recovery and concentration-controlled ADH signalling to conserve water; a drink finally replaces the deficit. Optional humid-air comparison shows that sweat can cost water while providing less cooling.

**Combination angle:** a response that solves one problem alters another system's working conditions. Different structures reuse interfaces and flow, eventually becoming one model. **Worthwhile angle:** homeostasis has costs and environmental limits; an organ's function cannot be understood alone. **Hook:** cooling succeeds, but the story is not finished. **Risk:** too many supplied organ modules; false equivalence of skin, lung and gut surfaces; humidity as an optional observation may steal attention from assembly. The renal work could be a late technical detour.

#### I — Three doors into one body

Six short chapters, roughly 24–32 combinations. Start with blood, pumping chambers, vessels and a living tissue bed. Assemble a closed circuit serving cells. Independently construct ventilation plus a gas-exchange surface, digestion plus absorption, and kidney filtration plus recovery. Connect all three to the same circulation. Add skin and two specific regulatory paths: temperature sensing changes sweat/skin blood flow; fluid concentration changes kidney water recovery. A warm walk and a subsequent drink make those previously separate constructions cooperate, reducing the board to one responding body.

**Combination angle:** familiar separate systems converge through one circulation and later alter one another's conditions. **Worthwhile angle:** the protected environment around cells depends on several controlled crossings; ordinary acts recruit a whole body. **Hook:** each crossing offers a different surprise, culminating in coordinated response. **Risk:** a dozen preliminary organ fittings could be an entrance fee for the interesting last third. The first circuit may already be familiar to the audience, and supplied special parts weaken the promise of making everything from a few basics. A correct body catalogue may still be tedious.

### School-level reset: three candidates

The user rejected the kidney prototype as too advanced and requested a grades 8–10 organ/system approach. The following comparison supersedes the earlier selection as active direction.

#### A — Lift a glass

Start with arm bones, a joint and two opposing muscles. Construct an arm that can bend and straighten; the useful discovery is that muscles pull rather than push. Independently connect heart/blood vessels and lungs/breathing muscles. Add a gut supplying nutrients and kidneys removing wastes. Join these supply routes to the earlier arm, then connect brain, nerves and eyes so it can act on what it sees. Skin adds a temperature response. After a warm period of activity, the completed body reaches for a glass and drinks: sensing, movement, blood flow, fuel, oxygen and exchange now serve one familiar act.

Around 16–18 actions in five small chapters. Early demonstrations establish mechanical possibilities, not isolated organs living without support. Ordinary unmodelled supporting anatomy is present. Detailed endocrine, renal and molecular mechanisms are outside scope.

Worthwhile: ordinary actions depend on many systems; the body's apparent independence relies on continuous internal cooperation and external supplies. Hook: progressively making a familiar body capable of something, with immediate arm movement before wider assembly. Risk: numerous organ hookups may become a checklist, and the ending could merely certify what the audience already expects.

#### B — Keep going

Start with bones, joints and muscles, then build circulation and breathing to support activity. A short walk reveals that moving muscles need supplies and produce heat. Attach the gut to replenish supplies over time, kidneys to remove wastes, and skin/control connections to lose heat. The end is one body continuing to work through changing conditions.

Around 12–16 actions in four chapters. Regulation is shown through changes in breathing, heartbeat and sweat, without named hormones or cellular chemistry. No resource depletion meters or artificial emergencies.

Worthwhile: life involves continuous adjustment, and solving one need can create another. Hook: a construction is tested, then improved for a concrete reason. Risk: the model may appear able to walk before the player assembles the neural/energy support it needed; adding each missing organ after a problem appears could become contrived repair. It has less emphasis on sensory coordination.

#### C — From a sandwich to a hand

Start with food, stomach and intestines. Build digestion and absorption, circulation, and lungs supplying oxygen. Deliver both nutrients and oxygen to a muscle attached to an arm. Connect an opposing muscle, then nerves and senses, until the body can lift its next bite. Finish with one body whose visible movement is connected to earlier invisible supply work.

Around 12–16 actions in four chapters. Explain stored fuel so a fresh bite is not portrayed as instantly powering the next movement. No ATP, digestive enzymes by name, glucose cotransport or detailed kidney physiology.

Worthwhile: food, air and movement belong to one connected process rather than separate textbook chapters. Hook: a familiar meal acquires an unfamiliar causal story. Risk: it can become a guided tour of food's journey, with early digestion postponing construction of the body; the cyclical ending might be a neat illustration rather than a reason to play.

## Prototype log and earlier score tables


These chronological notes describe successive drafts. The sole current manuscript is [anatomy.md](anatomy.md); earlier manuscripts have been removed. Earlier paths, commands and counts refer to the versions tested at the time.

**Direction after the initial prototype:** the user rejected the kidney-centred prototype below as far too advanced and authorized a new grades 8–10 organ/system story. See [School-level reset](#prototype-school-level-reset). Earlier findings remain historical evidence about a different manuscript; the brief and arc at that stage described *Lift a glass*.

### Method and scope

Started 2026-11-11. User requested source research, repeated three-variant brainstorming with blind review, narrative and combination review, and iterative Terra `--fast` playtests. Gameplay only. This is a development study, not an accepted catalogue story or a learning-effectiveness experiment.

Fresh reviewers receive only the current assigned artifact and the applicable criteria. They do not receive the author's preferred answer, previous reviews or this log. Concept comparisons ask for a ranking, a concrete promising action, a concrete failure mode and a reject-all option. Raw inputs and outputs are disposable under `runs/undissection/`; this file retains assignments, decisions and limits. Player/grader deliverables follow the repository's separate retention rule.

### Source preparation

Completed source preparation: 23 complete OpenStax sections (92,841 whitespace-delimited words, including metadata, exercises and MathML; 377 glossary definitions), original CNXML plus derived HTML/Markdown, pinned to a publisher snapshot explicitly licensed CC BY 4.0. Full local texts are ignored and deletable; copyrighted textbook/workbook references have original study notes instead of full reproductions. The source index records exactly what was read and saved, including access failures and known source inaccuracies. The downloaded edition predates the publisher's later licence change.

### Concept round 1

Two fresh independent reviewers received only Round 1 A/B/C, the grade 10–12 audience, combination format, text-only constraint and worthwhile/hook criteria. Both ranked **B > C > A**. They identified selective filtration/recovery and the distinction between gut contents and internal environment as promising enacted insights. Both warned that four tissue families could support arbitrary organ lookup, a generic regulation tile could conceal the actual causal chain, and walking could be declared before its supporting physiology existed.

Decision: carry boundary/exchange reasoning forward, keep a familiar bodily consequence as the payoff, and compare concrete sequences rather than declare B accepted. The suggested human studies and animated quantities were not performed; current evidence is concept criticism only. Round 2 explicitly respects the existing engine's text consequences rather than presuming a numerical simulation.

### Concept round 2

Two new blind reviewers received D/E/F alone with the same audience and the actual binary/text engine limits. Both ranked **E > F > D**. Selective recovery offered a stronger enacted reversal than simply attaching three organ interfaces to circulation. Both identified the cost of cooling and the limits of conservation as a possible continuation. They warned against water being actively pumped, dry weather being treated as the cause of dehydration, and heart-plus-vessels construction being an entry fee to an interesting later chapter.

Decision: compare a compact water-balance episode, a cooling-led episode and a broader whole-body arc before committing scope. Do not add timers, quantities, blocked-combination feedback or human-study claims that the existing test does not provide. Keep the user's broader undissection ambition visible, while testing the smallest complete arc that can earn expansion.

### Concept round 3 and scope decision

Two new blind reviewers received only G/H/I and the common audience/engine constraints. Both ranked **G > H > I**. They valued filtration → recovery → conditional conservation → replacement as a causal arc. They cautioned that specialised starter parts do not amount to constructing a whole body, that a supplied intestine attached to a body could be an administrative fitting, and that the ADH pathway could become a vocabulary gate. The proposed human comparisons were not conducted.

Adopt **G, A glass of water**, as a small complete episode before broader anatomy. Preserve the larger “few → many → one body” ambition as an expansion hypothesis, not an achieved result. Four connected systems can reveal interdependence without paying an entrance fee of familiar heart/artery fittings. The current prototype still has to earn those connections through play.

### Narrative review assignment

Fresh reviewers receive the current narrative arc, active brief and standing authoring criteria, without concept rankings or this history. Ask whether the questions and player actions drive the next chapter, whether the ending depends on earlier work, and where an apparently meaningful construction is only a label. Separate factual mechanism review from narrative judgment.

#### Outcomes and adopted changes

The narrative reviewer retained the conservation-versus-replacement hook but did not endorse four chapters on outline evidence alone. It asked for a quick filtration reversal, a bridge from fixed recovery to variable water needs, distinct contributions from gradient and permeability, and an ending where absorption changes the existing feedback response automatically. It identified the counterfactual test: would a ready-made kidney make the ending equally understandable and satisfying?

The independent physiology reviewer found no premise blocker, conditional on preserving circulation, distinct blood/tubular paths, ordinary proximal recovery before ADH adjustment, a maintained gradient, hypothalamic synthesis versus posterior-pituitary release, evaporative cooling and a modest deficit. Adopted these constraints in the 15-combination draft. A supplied circulation remains the same identity through its early perfusion and later convergence. The finale changes the internal condition; it does not ask the player to switch off ADH or declare perfect recovery after one glass.

The current draft uses a single plain one-pixel SVG for the parser's required image fields. All playable items are text. No anatomical artwork, icon design or graphics evaluation was performed.

### Combination and authenticity reviews

Fresh reviewers saw the first complete 15-recipe manuscript and active brief, without the earlier reviews. The gameplay reviewer retained the glucose/recovery opening and conservation-limit payoff. Adopted: name the gradient-producing input as its loop/vessel assembly; avoid a second circulation hookup that only changes viewing scale; give the intestinal-wall construction an immediate crossing-versus-transport explanation; narrow the hypothalamus primitive so the posterior pituitary adds its release site. The independent authenticity reviewer found the scoped physiology defensible and flagged physical identity in the gradient input and the intestinal wall apparently becoming water. The revised latter result retains the absorbing wall before it joins the body. Added the verified primary reference for anticipatory drinking signals and qualified volume effects as increasingly important with larger losses.

A separate fresh reader received only the public chapter-two scene, goals and six item names (compatibility hints omitted for this isolated probe). First it chose to connect the recovering renal unit directly to the loop, while acknowledging uncertainty about the accompanying vessels. Only after that answer did it receive the proposed loop-plus-vessels pair; it predicted establishing/preserving the gradient and distinguished the still-needed adjustable wall. Adopted the plausible first choice as an alternative assembly order, rather than blocking it for a preferred sequence. This probe supports local intelligibility, not sustained enjoyment.

The resulting pre-playtest draft has **16 authored recipes, 14 required combinations, four playable chapters and one ending**. There are two equivalent orders for the loop/vessel assembly. The additional route replaces the removed redundant circulation hookup; no extra completion steps were added for score or length.

### Baseline Terra fast campaign

[Report](playtests/2026.11.11_02.29.14/report.md), manuscript SHA-256 `f1119215e9cc890ac03de515e534b4c04e3d0a8ced452f550db39d1d2efbcde0`. Three fresh Terra Medium players per chapter, twelve fresh Terra Medium graders and four fresh Terra Medium chapter synthesizers. The adapter actually selected `gpt-6.1-terra`, effort `medium`. All twelve records completed and passed frozen-engine replay/grade validation. Three external worker slots ran the dependency pipeline; final report assembly occurred immediately after the last synthesis. Additional guidance and model/timing metadata are retained in each chapter report. No continuous human or detailed-mode test was performed.

```text
Chapter  Title                                  Fun           Worthwhile  Hook
1        What a filter lets through             not measured  2/3         medium (3)
2        The same kidney, a different output    not measured  4/5         low (1), medium (2)
3        Cooling costs water                    not measured  3/3         low (1), medium (2)
4        A drink reaches the cells              not measured  1.7/3       low (3)
```

The lower-half worthwhile screen selects chapters 1 and 4; the no-high-hook screen selects all four. Chapter 3's full worthwhile count does not establish a compelling game: all three players found the connection to the kidney predictable, while valuing what its explanation taught. Across the campaign, the glucose reversal, gradient-plus-permeability distinction, conservation limit and changed renal response to a drink were valued. Repeated fittings and missing mechanism explanations weakened interest. Normal compatible-pair hints are part of the game; these findings do not justify hiding them or manufacturing puzzles.

#### Revision hypothesis

1. Bundle the initial filter/tubule plumbing so the first action immediately reveals useful glucose entering filtrate. Preserve the separate blood and tubular routes in the supplied assembly.
2. Bundle the loop/vessel preparation into one concrete countercurrent assembly, and explain how unequal limb permeability, salt transport and accompanying blood flow maintain a gradient when it joins the earlier renal unit. Remove one low-value fitting, rather than multiply specialist labels.
3. Keep the valued cooling/conservation passage. Offer a choice between dry and humid warm settings with unreplaced sweat loss. The choice must alter evaporative cooling while preserving the common water deficit, giving the environment an actual physiological role rather than introducing an arbitrary fork.
4. Bundle the intestinal lining/capillaries and spend the remaining actions on absorption and its consequence. Explain solute transport and osmotic water movement, addressing the missing mechanism rather than simply repeating that water crosses.
5. Remove the empty ending chapter. Structural analysis showed that it cleared the final body from the current board (zero items at that chapter), contrary to the intended convergence. The final result should remain the single body; no extra victory object or action is needed.

Run fresh narrative and authenticity-director reviews, then retest all four affected chapters with identical guidance, model, effort and mode. This is a test of less preparatory work plus more substantial relationships, not an attempt to inflate a decision score. If hooks remain weak, retain that finding and do not expand anatomy coverage to conceal it.

#### Independent director review and revision 1

Fresh story and authenticity directors received only the current baseline manuscript, active brief and relevant standing/source guidance, not the results above. The story director independently recommended compressing preparatory assemblies, retaining the gradient/permeability construction and the conservation-limit turn, and returning the final response to the initial glucose-recovery thread. It considered the initial filter/tubule orientation defensible; the actual unanimous reports of low value were the reason to bundle it anyway. The authenticity director found no essential inaccuracy and requested that the loop/vessel gradient be stated as depending on established tubular and blood flow. The revision includes that condition.

Revision 1 has 13 authored recipes and 11 required actions across four playable chapters. It starts with three items, peaks at five along the debugger route, and ends on one body. The earlier alternate loop-fitting order disappears with the bundled preparation; the new dry/humid alternatives change the resulting condition. The hormone-release branch remains separately constructible because its connection to the kidney received positive local value, despite the director's optional suggestion to bundle it too. The ending explicitly keeps glucose recovery and waste excretion operating while extra water conservation eases.

### Revision 1: all-chapter retest

[Report](playtests/2026.11.11_02.41.16/report.md), SHA-256 `8baff46cadae24e33f5943d4e3473377b6516305e3ee05646f42884ee0316268`. Same guidance, mode, model, effort, three-player design and independent grading/synthesis as baseline. All twelve playthroughs completed and verified. These are new participants, not returning players experiencing improvement.

```text
Chapter  Title                                  Fun before    Fun after     Worthwhile before  Worthwhile after
1        What a filter lets through             not measured  not measured  2/3                2/2
2        The same kidney, a different output    not measured  not measured  4/5                4/4
3        Cooling costs water                    not measured  not measured  3/3                2.7/3
4        A drink reaches the cells              not measured  not measured  1.7/3              2/2
```

The three preparatory cuts reduced the completion route from 14 to 11 actions. The credited-action counts for chapters 1 and 2 stayed the same; their fractions improved because low-value work was removed, not because more value was demonstrated. Chapter 4's explicit mechanism explanation received credit from all three players. Chapter 3 fell slightly on the broad worthwhile measure despite the new environmental choice; one player found the skin/kidney hookup repetitive. Preserve that contrary result.

Two players chose the humid walk to test limited evaporation; one chose the dry walk and predicted cooling. All three graders credited that choice as meaningful in the fast diagnostic. This is evidence of one reasoned comparison opportunity, not a human-fun result or a substantial replay loop. Hook was medium for all players in chapters 1–3, and low/medium (2 low, 1 medium) in chapter 4. **No high hook was reported.**

Chapter 4 trials used the debugger's dry-route checkpoint history. They did not continue the chapter 3 participants' chosen paths. The humid branch's convergence was checked by the shared-engine replay and factual reviewers, not by a continuous player experiencing that branch through the drink.

### Final director reviews and focused ending retest

Two fresh directors saw the revised manuscript and active brief, without playtest scores, history or earlier criticism. The narrative director recommended **retain as a small teaching prototype; do not expand as-is**. It valued interdependence and the limits of conservation but judged the weather fork an authored observation rather than a substantial replay hook. It requested a concrete change in urine output in the final result. The authenticity director found no factual blockers across all 13 recipes, including both environmental paths and convergence; it cautioned that “Regulated kidney” includes a brain-to-kidney relationship whose separate locations must remain clear in any future representation. No artwork is authorized here.

Adopted the final-output clarification: as extra recovery eases, more water can leave as urine and the urine becomes less concentrated than during the strongest conservation response. Retested chapter 4 only with three new players, graders and a new synthesizer; other chapters were unchanged.

[Focused report](playtests/2026.11.11_02.46.52/report.md), final manuscript SHA-256 `04b834d57f51b19663a0262620fca34a66298dc03c296a57050e64e2c60dda30`.

```text
Chapter  Title                       Fun before    Fun after     Worthwhile before  Worthwhile after
4        A drink reaches the cells   not measured  not measured  2/2                2/2
```

Before is revision 1; after is the focused final run. Hook remained low/medium (2 low, 1 medium). The clarification improved explicitness, not the measured hook verdict. Players still found the whole-body response useful while describing a prescribed action followed by a substantial explanation. Across all three campaigns there were **27 actual chapter playthroughs, 27 fresh independent grades and nine fresh chapter syntheses**, all Terra Medium, with 81 accepted gameplay actions in total. These totals describe separate local trials, never a continuous 81-action story.

### Current judgment and stopping point

The worthwhile **subject** is established enough for a small prototype: useful substances must be recovered after filtration; a gradient and a permeable route do different jobs; cooling costs water and depends on the environment; conservation cannot replace a loss; absorption changes the response of the same body. The combinations make some relationships concrete, especially the glucose reversal, gradient/permeability construction and environmental choice.

The **hook remains unproven and weak in these AI reports**. Strong credited-learning counts coexist with low or conditional desire to continue. Much of the reward still comes from the explanations; do not infer that this format has an advantage over an equally good illustrated reading. The target age group, retained learning, cumulative attachment, browser dragging and human enjoyment were not evaluated. Fast-mode Fun is not measured.

Retain this small complete episode as a reviewable development result. Do not expand into a full anatomy catalogue, add more regulation chains, commission art or promote it to the published catalogue on this evidence. The larger undissection idea remains interesting, but this particular construction pattern has not earned that expansion. A human reading/playing this compact draft would supply more useful direction than another round of optimizing its AI scores.

### Final verification and retention

- `npm run lint -- projects/undissection/undissection.md --details`: zero errors, zero warnings on the final version.
- `npm run analyze -- projects/undissection/undissection.md --json --details`: complete legal route, 25 items (13 primitive, 12 derived), 13 authored recipes, 11 completion actions, four playable chapters; starting boards 3, 5, 5, 3 and route peaks no higher than 5. The draft is deliberately much smaller than Cookbook/Settlement; count comparisons do not establish quality.
- Explicit shared-engine replays selected each of the dry and humid branches. Both completed in 11 combinations with exactly one available item on the final current board: **Body responding to a drink**. This checks the two defined paths, not every possible action ordering.
- A Chromium smoke check opened the local draft at a 390 × 844 viewport, entered through Start/Begin and found the opening items; HTTP 200, no page errors. It was not a visual-design, geometry or human-usability evaluation. The existing development server was left alone.
- All 27 actual sessions and independent grades passed frozen-runtime replay, protocol coverage and arithmetic validation. Report syntheses preserve original graders' classifications; mean worthwhile counts and aggregate hook labels were checked against the attributed details. Run reports were mechanically assembled from marked chapter syntheses.
- Local source conversions retain publisher attribution, licence, source URLs and checksums. Full downloads remain ignored and deletable under `sources/local/`. No copyrighted textbook/workbook was retained in full. Selected response arrays and chapter/run reports stay with the project; disposable sessions, prompts, intermediate grades, logs and analysis dumps are removed after verification.

No application code, production story, shared UX rule, published host or artwork set changed. This active project is not ready for promotion or archiving as an accepted story.


<a id="prototype-school-level-reset"></a>

### School-level reset

The user judged the renal story far beyond the desired audience, then authorized a new attempt for grades 8–10. This is a replacement of the active manuscript, brief and arc, not a claim that simpler wording makes the renal mechanisms suitable. Earlier scores above apply only to that superseded draft. The current project remains active; no catalogue promotion or graphics is authorized.

#### Concepts and scope

Two fresh blind reviewers compared three new candidates: **A, Lift a glass**; **B, Keep going**; **C, From a sandwich to a hand**. They received only the candidate sketches, audience and format constraints. Rankings were A > C > B and C > A > B. A supplied the strongest immediate action, opposing muscular pulls; C supplied a clearer convergence of food, air and blood. Both doubted whether a claimed continuation of activity could be earned by a text-only engine, and whether kidneys and temperature regulation belonged in a single reach.

Adopt A's mechanical opening and C's supply connection. Put sensory control before the supply chapters, so the brain is not a final life switch. Omit renal and temperature detours. The resulting fourteen-combination arc uses bones, muscles, nerves, eyes, lungs, heart, blood vessels, stomach and small intestine. It ends with one body lifting a glass. It models selected relationships and supporting assemblies, not exhaustive human anatomy or resurrection.

A separate source/scope reviewer grounded the organ-system level in NGSS MS-LS1-3 and HS-LS1-2, identified stored-fuel and muscle-braking cautions, and added licensed OpenStax 9.4 and 11.1 to the earlier source snapshot. The local library now contains 25 sections, 101,716 whitespace-delimited words, 418 glossary definitions and 114 figure descriptions. These remain author reference, not a student reading list. See the source index for the pinned license and retrieval limits.

#### Fresh manuscript reviews

Two new independent reviewers read the school manuscript and active brief without earlier reviews or playtest records. One assessed age prerequisites and narrative; the other assessed physiology and combination relationships. Neither identified a blocking physiological defect; both found the school level plausible with prior basic biology. That is adult expert judgment, not demonstrated student accessibility.

Adopted fixes: replace “Air has to reach the arm” with “Oxygen has to reach the arm”; put the complete heart → lungs → heart → body → heart route in required text; explain the thin-walled meeting place between air and blood; define cells, tendons and nutrients in mandatory result paragraphs; replace “contract,” “levers” and “sensory information” with ordinary descriptions. The required food result explicitly mentions the delay in absorption and stored fuel. The ending now discovers that holding the glass still also requires muscle pulls and energy, reconnecting it to the first chapter.

The reviewers independently expected sight and meal preparation to be the weakest actions. Both serve explanatory contrasts, but their actual experienced value remains for the playtest to assess. No harder guessing or extra anatomy steps were added.

#### School baseline assignment

Four chapters; three fresh Terra Medium players per chapter, twelve fresh Terra Medium independent graders, and one fresh Terra Medium synthesis reviewer per chapter. Actual adapter selection: `gpt-6.1-terra`, `model_reasoning_effort="medium"`, with shell, apps and web disabled. The CLI model label alone is not relied upon. Each invocation is ephemeral and receives only its public assignment packet; players retain their own chapter's exact public history through subsequent packets. Synthesis intentionally sees its three records and grades. No reviewer receives earlier findings or a future recipe.

Mode: `--fast`; fun is not measured. Guidance identifies grades 8–10 and familiar biology, excludes assumed specialist anatomy/cellular chemistry, and requests honest assessment. Its exact wording is retained in every chapter report. A three-worker completion-driven pipeline immediately schedules grading and synthesis when prerequisites complete, then mechanically assembles the report. Whole-story chapter coverage uses fresh participants at each checkpoint, not a single person's cumulative attachment.

Baseline manuscript SHA-256: `11efb33e980043fa16ed86e12de0a6d650a4599a0da481f5645bf49224fa07c0`. Verified outcomes follow below.

#### School baseline outcomes and revision

[Baseline report](playtests/2026.11.11_09.33.43/report.md): all twelve chapter assignments completed and replay-verified; 42 gameplay actions in total. Three fresh records per chapter. Chapter synthesis credited the following average counts, using the standing either/or worthwhile definition; `--fast` does not measure fun.

| Chapter | Worthwhile | Hook judgments |
|---|---:|---|
| A muscle can only pull | 1.7/3 | 1 medium, 2 high |
| Where should the hand go? | 2/3 | 1 medium, 2 high |
| Oxygen has to reach the arm | 2.7/3 | 3 high |
| More than an arm | 5/5 | 3 medium |

The first chapter exposed an authoring defect: the three slot conditions were written within one prose block, so the parser treated them as literal text and showed all the muscle explanations when the empty arm was created. This made later fittings repetitive and could imply muscles were already fitted. Lint alone did not catch that semantic presentation mistake. Separate repeated `Arm` declarations now give each condition its own wonder block. Direct engine inspection confirms one unconditional reading and three correctly gated readings, and no tendon/muscle explanation on the initial joint construction.

All three air-chapter players identified the missing link between breathing muscles and changing chest space. The revised result explains the diaphragm pulling down, other muscles lifting ribs, lungs expanding, and passive recoil during quiet breathing. No additional anatomy operands or technical pathways were introduced.

The ending's holding-still discovery was valued by all three players. The supply/control join was predictable or repetitive for two, while still clarifying distinct jobs. Retain that necessary binary convergence and its explanation; do not add arbitrary choices simply to increase decision counts. Hook remained medium at the ending, so this is not evidence of a uniformly compelling story.

The second campaign repeats all four chapters with fresh participants because every later checkpoint includes the revised opening history, and chapters 3–4 also include the changed breathing explanation. This is a specific exposure change, not routine repeated testing. Revised manuscript SHA-256: `8defaa3a50ce43a0f2dd2f6f17097fed93eafe33a686a303f21a70190fa1532e`. Same guidance, models, mode and pipeline.


#### School revision outcomes and current judgment

[Revised report](playtests/2026.11.11_09.38.22/report.md), matching the current manuscript: twelve completed, replay-verified chapter assignments and twelve independent grades, with four fresh chapter syntheses. Forty-two actions; three fresh players per chapter.

| Chapter | Baseline worthwhile | Revised worthwhile | Revised hook judgments |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| A muscle can only pull | 1.7/3 | 2/3 | 1 medium, 2 high |
| Where should the hand go? | 2/3 | 3/3 | 3 high |
| Oxygen has to reach the arm | 2.7/3 | 3/3 | 1 medium, 2 high |
| More than an arm | 5/5 | 5/5 | 3 medium |

These are averages of credited actions and total actions separately, not percentages or learning gains. All independent graders classified the recorded fast-menu decisions as non-meaningful; worthwhile credit comes from evidenced value in results. Fun is not measured by this mode. The changes in tiny independent samples do not isolate a causal effect of revision, and high hook in earlier chapters does not establish cumulative whole-story attachment. The ending retained medium, conditional interest despite its valued payoff.

The reveal-timing defect is gone. Bending-muscle installation now produces the explanation that all three opening players named most worthwhile. The straightening installation remains predictable and repeats the tendon definition, which is kept so either fitting order is understandable. The breathing result now explains muscular expansion and quiet recoil; this revision's players did not repeat the earlier missing-muscle-mechanism criticism. The gas-exchange convergence remained valued by all three.

All three final-chapter players valued the discovery that holding still takes continuing muscle effort. Digestion versus absorption and stored fuel also earned specific positive reactions. Remaining questions concern how position is sensed and how oxygen supports energy release. Keep those as known limits of this selective organ/system model; do not add microscopic mechanisms simply to satisfy adult curiosity. Required assembly remains predictable, particularly at the supply/control join.

**Current judgment:** retain this small, playable school-level prototype. Its connections and ordinary-action payoff justify trying it with the intended audience, but the AI evidence does not establish that it teaches or entertains students. It is a guided construction toy with worthwhile explanatory results, not yet a strong inference game. Further expansion, a full-body inventory, homeostasis episodes or artwork should depend on the user's response and target-age evidence, not these scores alone.

#### Final independent review

A fresh final reviewer read only the revised manuscript, active brief, school scope and authoring criteria, without playtests or previous reviews. It found no blocking biological inaccuracy or causal gap at the intended scale and judged the four-part arc coherent. It identified holding still as the strongest ordinary-experience payoff and the school prerequisites as plausible with basic biology.

Its main limitation is consequential: successful assembly does not show that the learner can reconstruct the explanation. Its suggested next evidence is transfer to another familiar action. The organ–tissue–cell hierarchy is only partly stated; that remains outside this episode's learning target rather than becoming another required label sequence. These are expert judgments, not a target-age study.

#### Final verification and retention

The current manuscript passes story lint with zero errors and one reviewed mixed-fate warning: the arm remains while muscles are fitted, then transforms into the guided arm. That is intentional identity progression. The analyzer finds four chapters, fourteen combinations, a fourteen-action completion route and at most six arriving items. Direct engine runs checked both muscle-fitting orders crossed with both breathing/circulation preparation orders; all four routes complete in fourteen actions with exactly one available result, `Body lifting a glass`.

A Chromium check at 390 × 844 loaded the draft and performed the opening drag combinations. The empty arm shows only its joint explanation, the first muscle shows its own result, and the second fitting shows the opposing-pair result. No browser errors occurred. No artwork or screenshots were produced.

Both school campaigns retain their requested original response arrays, independent chapter reports and mechanically assembled reports. All 24 response arrays were compared with their frozen session replies, including harness-owned look flags; 84 actions and all independent grading totals match. Each session completed and replay verification passed; hashes and identical guidance were checked, along with exact copying of the eight marked chapter syntheses into the two assembled reports. Detailed action tables support the displayed worthwhile averages. Runtime snapshots, packets, CLI adapters, raw logs and temporary reviews are disposable scratch and were removed after these checks. The licensed full source texts remain ignored under `sources/local/` for the ongoing development project.


<a id="prototype-saved-progress-mismatch-reported-after-the-rewrite"></a>

#### Saved-progress mismatch reported after the rewrite

On 2026-11-11 the user reported the continuation-removal error for the opening arm pair and brain/nerves, both offered together in chapter 1. They confirmed having opened an earlier draft at the same browser address and that **Restart story** resolved the error. The current manuscript's fresh initial inventory has only the arm construction; brain/nerves arrive in chapter 2.

Investigation checked every combination/advance transition in the fresh logical story (21 distinct logical states, 40 directed transitions, no failures) and a complete 14-combination Chromium playthrough (no errors). A small separate reproduction confirmed that `reconcileLiveStory` can preserve a consumed ingredient across a changed recipe, leaving an already-impossible saved position while still offering other compatible pairs. Those pairs then raise the generic continuation-removal error. The user's exact local snapshot was not inspected; their restart outcome and mixed inventory are consistent with this restoration path. This does not establish a defect in the current fresh manuscript, but exposes a browser restoration gap and a misleading error. No application code was changed during this diagnosis. The unresolved recovery work is tracked in the cross-project backlog. Disposable reproduction scripts were removed.

Round one accepts sensible preparation orders; round two tests independent compound fittings; round three restores progressive skin and tests a persistent resident cell producing repair material. The skin compound was rejected. Vessel and lung compounds remain provisional: they remove the observed ordering failures without demonstrating improved Fun. Skin recovers round one's 7/8 worthwhile count but keeps medium hook. Repair improves the counts, while all three players miss the cell context for export; its hook distribution remains two medium and one high. The study preserves that friction and the alternative skin route's missed wonder as unresolved weaknesses.

Across the records, applying a built capability and showing a distinct consequence is stronger evidence of value than simply renaming or attaching a prepared part. The independent final analyst also finds counterexamples: some fittings add a useful distinction, and an obvious action can have a valued outcome. Meaningful credit on an unsuccessful hypothesis must not be attributed to the automatically supplied successful operation. These small unpaired AI samples do not establish a universal ranking of combination forms.

No replacement grading attempts were needed. The final manuscript matches round three's source hash, passes lint with zero errors and five intentional compound-transition warnings, and has 70 authored rules / 62 required actions across ten chapters. No engine, UI, graphics, browser, screenshot or publication work was performed. Requested arrays and reports remain; disposable scratch is removed after verification. The local licensed source library remains for ongoing development.

## Ten-chapter expansion and fast trials


This is a historical study of earlier ten-chapter manuscripts, which have been removed. Continue development in [anatomy.md](anatomy.md); the [full-story study](#full-story-development) covers its current 30-chapter form. Original filenames and scores below identify the versions tested.

An active exploration following the user's favorable response to *Lift a glass*. Intended eventual scale: about 200 combinations in 20–30 chapters. Immediate experiment: ten varied chapters. The position of this passage in a longer game is deliberately open. The [design brief](../../stories/anatomy/design-brief.md) owns accepted intent; the [narrative arc](#narrative-arc) describes the current experiment. No graphics, screenshots or browser verification are in scope.

### Compound finding

The existing format accepts a fully fitted compound as a qualified operand in an ordinary combination. The result can be a single ordinary item in the same chapter. This does not happen automatically on fitting the last slot, and slots do not automatically carry into the new identity. The draft uses real additional contributions: contractile structures complete a muscle-cell model, and pacemaker cells give the fitted heart a rhythmic activity. No fake replacement primitive or engine change is needed. The specification owns these rules; this is their application to this story.

### Concept comparisons

Two blind review rounds compared three proposals each. Reviewers received only the current candidates and standing criteria, not prior judgments or the author's preference. Their answers are scope criticism, not playtest results.

| Round | Candidates | Independent rankings | Adopted finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A: build upward from cells; B: follow a hand's needs inward/outward; C: a later passage centred on repair | B > C > A; B > A > C | Start with a recognizable bodily question. Keep repair as a bounded test of a different kind of cellular work. An uninterrupted hierarchy of parts is not sufficient meaning. |
| 2 | A: ten chapters following a hand through cellular work, supply and repair; B: three cell chapters before the arm; C: ten chapters on differently arranged boundaries | A > C > B; A > C > B | Develop A with variable chapter lengths. Remove generic “support” or “zoom” fees; use specific cell identities and the same body's skin. Keep the ordinary-action ending brief. |

Both rounds questioned some proposed steps within these particular outlines. The current scope is not a quota. Particularly exposed material: organelle fittings, fuel preparation, repeated transport hookups and extra repair states. Reviewer recommendations to bundle these are hypotheses to test against actual combinations, not automatic cuts to make an outline short.

### Initial adopted decisions

- Keep the previous fourteen-action story at its existing path. The expansion uses a new manuscript URL, avoiding the known old-progress mismatch.
- Model muscle cells specifically in the opening. Their protein threads add a real capability when the compound becomes an ordinary item. The later skin repair uses resident skin cells, not those muscle cells repurposed.
- Keep ATP local and continually remade. Explain ATP production outside mitochondria as well as their aerobic contribution; omit named reaction chains and fixed yields.
- Let the returning blood circuit receive lung and gut interfaces; do not create a new independent circulation for every tissue.
- Let a small accidental scrape affect the same skin built earlier. Temporary sealing and tissue replacement are different jobs; response phases overlap and the hand remains broadly functional.
- Put grounding, sources and limits in author-only NOTE blocks throughout the manuscript. Essential knowledge stays in visible result text.
- Do not implement dissection. A parts-kit output would currently be an administrative unpacking step without a demonstrated new understanding.

### Review of the actual arc and combinations

Two fresh reviewers received the 65-action manuscript, active brief and standing authoring/format criteria. The science reviewer additionally received the source-grounding document and checked primary references. Neither received the concept verdicts, narrative-arc document or playtests. These informed editorial reviews are distinct from the later packet-only player trials.

The scientific review found no blocking factual error: local ATP renewal, mitochondrial and non-mitochondrial ATP production, distinct cell identities and overlapping repair responses were accurately qualified. It recommended retaining the identity of the whole returning vessel circuit, making the sensory return from stretch receptors explicit, distinguishing local chemical repair signals from nerve signals, and reducing vague collagen helper labels. Before the baseline, the circuit and stretch-sensor names were clarified and the scrape result explicitly identified clotting/cleanup components as already belonging to the body. Chemical-signal naming and late repair simplification remain concrete revision candidates.

The combination reviewer counted 58 explicit recipes and seven fittings. It judged all three compound transformations substantive: contractile threads add tension-producing organization, motor nerves add outgoing control, and pacemaker cells add rhythm. Its strongest explanatory relationships were permission versus force, opposing pulls, information versus supply, pump/valve/rhythm, air versus blood, digestion versus absorption and temporary seal versus rebuilt tissue.

The main criticisms were delay and unnecessary packaging. The promised lift occurs only after all ten chapters; the reviewer proposed moving it before the injury. It proposed supplying cell fluid and nuclei rather than making their water/salts and DNA/envelope assemblies, retaining the cell's selective membrane and functional organelle contributions. It also challenged the brain/spinal-cord grouping, breathing-muscle grouping, vague extracellular helpers and the food-preparation preamble. These are candidates for evidence-led revision, not an instruction to delete every predictable combination. The baseline preserves them so the play records can identify their actual value or weakness.

### Breadth and connections for the longer game

A chapter earns additional length through a new explanatory relationship, not merely another named structure. The following map will be revised after the ten-chapter trial. Later passages are proposals, not authored recipes or evidence of play value.


The body plainly supplies enough material for roughly 200 combinations. The user corrected an earlier interpretation that turned local pacing criticisms into doubt about that abundance. The ten drafted chapters are one narrow passage through movement, supply and repair; they do not sample all major systems or concerns. At the first draft’s density, thirty chapters would already contain about 195 combinations. The design task is **selection and connection**, not proving anatomical scarcity wrong.

The map below identifies additional explanatory relationships. Arrows are proposed constructions, not final recipes. Counts indicate possible local depth, not ceilings on the subject or evidence that every proposed step already works. New scientific details require source checking before authorship.

| Possible further question | Candidate combination relationships | What would make it worthwhile | Main risk / provisional room |
|---|---|---|---|
| How can the hand act before the next meal? | Absorbed sugar + liver storage → stored fuel; stored fuel + demand signal → available fuel | The body bridges gaps between supply and use; an arrival route is not the whole supply system. | Another transport hookup unless storage/release has a consequence; 5–7 actions. |
| How does working muscle change breathing? | Active tissue + continuing metabolism → changed blood gases; sensed change + breathing muscles → adjusted ventilation | A working part changes the conditions that the larger body must handle. | Abstract “need” tokens or a second breathing lesson; 6–8. |
| How can the body lose heat while keeping its core warm? | Skin circulation + heat transport → warm surface; sweat + evaporation → heat loss; temperature sensing + effectors → regulation | A boundary can exchange heat selectively; maintaining conditions requires feedback. | “Heat loss + heat production = balance” would omit regulation; 8–10. |
| How does the body keep water without keeping all waste? | Filtered fluid + selective return → reclaimed supplies; remaining fluid + exit route → excretion | Removing everything is as harmful as removing nothing; selective exchange serves the whole. | Return to the rejected nephron syllabus; 7–9, using bundled kidney work. |
